**How the WSLENV invariant changes — and this is the part that must not be got wrong.** §2a's invariant today is absolute: *"a lane path is never present in a spawn env that takes the WSL branch."* S9e **narrows** it rather than deleting it, and the narrowing is the whole slice: *a **Windows** lane path is never present in a spawn env that takes the WSL branch; a **Linux** lane path is present there and is exported through `WSLENV` exactly as the per-account WSL path is today* (`local-pty-provider.ts:762-764`, daemon twin `pty-subprocess.ts:762-764`) **[code]**. Concretely, the lane preparation gains a WSL arm that emits `wslLinuxConfigDir` instead of the host lane path — resolved the way `getPreparation`'s managed-WSL branch already resolves one, from the *target*, which `normalizeClaudeAccountSelectionTarget` gives `computeLaneLaunch` at the anchor (§2a) — while the **guard** on what actually crossed sits where the resolved shell does, in the lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm (`local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`, daemon twin `pty-subprocess.ts:723`), and becomes a **typed** assertion rather than a presence test — **scoped to lane-pinned spawns, not to WSL-branch spawns generally**: on a WSL-branch spawn **of a lane-pinned pane**, assert `env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` parses as a Linux path under that distro's *lane* root (`${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-lanes/${principalId}`), and refuse otherwise. **The scoping is load-bearing, not fastidiousness: stated over WSL-branch spawns generally the assertion breaks the feature S9e is layered on.** A lane-**less** WSL pane — the case this section itself keeps alive, below, as `credentialLane: 'wsl'` — carries `envPatch: { CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR: activeAccount.wslLinuxAuthPath }` (`runtime-auth-service.ts:683-697`, patch at `:693`) **[code]** over a path minted as `${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-accounts/${accountId}/auth` (`claude-accounts/service.ts:939`) **[code]**, which lives under the **account** root and not the lane root; a lane-root assertion applied to it would refuse **every managed-WSL account launch** on any host where S9e had shipped. **The lane-less predicate is therefore stated separately and is unchanged from today:** either no `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` at all, or the selected managed account's `wslLinuxAuthPath` under that distro's `claude-accounts` root — and what stays refused for it, exactly as before S9e, is a **Windows** path, so §2a's invariant is undiminished for every pane S9e does not provision. Deleting that assertion must turn a named test red — and the mutation proof is more important after S9e than before it, because before S9e the guard was "no lane path at all", which is trivially checkable, and after it the guard is "the *right kind* of lane path", which is not.

**Ownership, adopt and presence for a WSL pane.** Unchanged in shape, and that is the point of doing it as a slice rather than a special case: a WSL lane pane is bound to a principal at the funnel like any other pane (§2a), the ownership predicate and the adopt/materialize gate compare principals with no WSL arm at all, the wake and split inherit from the pane record, and the wipe is the principal's. What changes is one label: the pane renders `credentialLane: 'grant'` (owned, isolated) instead of rev 10's `'wsl'` (isolated per account, not per person), and `'wsl'` survives to mean exactly what it means today — a WSL pane whose distro has **no** lane, either because S9e is not shipped or because that distro was never provisioned. So a mixed host is legible on the presence row rather than ambiguous, and a person who provisions one distro and not another sees which is which.

**What S9e cannot do, stated.** A WSL distro is registered per **Windows user**, so on the one-account shared host both developers use the **same distro instance** with the same Linux uid — the §2m(6) residual, transplanted: Linux mode bits inside the distro protect a lane from nothing, because the peer is the same Linux user there too. S9e buys the same thing native lanes buy — authority isolation, not confidentiality — and it must be described that way in the UI, not as "your WSL agents are now private".

**Cost, honestly.** New: a `principal-credential-lane-wsl.ts` (~130) holding the Linux-side mint, the in-distro canonicalization and the chmod, plus a WSL arm on `computeLaneLaunch` (~40 lines) and a WSL arm on the lane writer and wipe (~50). Edits: `runtime-auth-service.ts:683-697` gains a lane-before-account branch for WSL targets, and the `credentialLane` union gains nothing (it reuses `'grant'`). Tests: the WSLENV narrowing with its mutation proof, in-distro containment (junction, escape, foreign marker) mirrored from `assertManagedAuthPath`'s shape, and a live step. **The real cost is not lines, it is that every live probe doubles**: each of §5's steps must be run again inside WSL, on the Windows host, because the classifier gap of §2m(5) means a pane can be host-classified one way and shell-classified the other. Budget it as **the largest slice after S9a**, and, if it is ever revived, do not ship it in the same change as S9a: the invariant it narrows is the one guarding a credential-directory mismatch, and it deserves to land alone.

## 3. Wire changes

All gated behind one new runtime capability, `agent.identity-lanes.v1`, declared beside its siblings (`src/shared/protocol-version.ts:77`) and added to `RUNTIME_CAPABILITIES` (`:111-150`) **[code]**; the client check follows `src/cli/handlers/account.ts:276-284`'s `capabilities?.includes(...)` precedent (check at `:278`) **[code]**. **That capability is host-advertised and client-checked, and it says one thing only — “this host has lanes”.** Rev 16 read it as the per-grant signal §2a's designation keys on, which round 5 refuted (it puts no per-grant value in the host's hands at all); rev 17 answered by adding a *client-declared* counterpart, `accounts.lane.push.v1`, as row 12. **Rev 19 deletes that row and the whole client-declared namespace with it:** the designation is a persisted human tick over already-bound grants, so the host needs no per-grant wire signal, and this table is back to **one** capability namespace running in **one** direction. Nothing in §2a, §2e or §2l now reads `ctx.clientCapabilities`.

**Rule classification note.** The three rules in `docs/reference/remote-wire-compatibility.md` do not cover new RPC *methods*. Rule 2 is about stream **opcodes**, and its rationale is that an unknown opcode "vanishes, and the feature behind it appears to hang" (`:31-44`) **[code]**. New methods do the opposite: `dispatcher.ts:44-54` and `:139-150` return `method_not_found` loudly **[code]**. So new methods are a **fourth case** the doc does not cover: failure is loud, but the client should still capability-gate so the user sees "update the host". Add that distinction to the wire doc alongside S9.

| # | Change | Rule | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `accounts.lane.push` (two-file blob + a bounded owner-authored `displayName` + `basedOnRefreshTokenSha256` + `reauthenticated` + `delegation`) | new-method (4th case) + capability gate | Schema-bounded and shape-validated before any write (§2b). Refused unless the caller's lane was **host-provisioned** (§2a), the principal **has** a designation at all (`accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated` otherwise — the **push** arm of the same code the provisioning refusal uses, §2a; reachable when the designated grant was unbound or revoked, §2a rule (iii)), the caller's grant **is** the principal's `delegatedGrantId` (§2e — `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated` otherwise), the account is not resident elsewhere (§2e), and the watermark matches (§2c/§2e). **The `delegatedGrantId` clause is flat, with no "unless the principal has no lease yet" carve-out, and rev 14 is what makes that shippable:** the lease is written by the provisioning consent action itself (§2a) and a lane is never created without one, so the bootstrap case the carve-out would have covered does not occur. The one later way a lane can hold no designation — rule (iii)'s unbind/revoke clear — is the preceding clause's `no_pusher_designated` refusal, not a carve-out in this one (rev 20). Rev 13 let the lease be "pinned from the `delegation` member of the first accepted push" — a client action writing an authority value — and that trap-door is deleted (§2e). `delegation` stays in the envelope as **detection** data for R-DR2 (§2e), never as a writer. `displayName` is the carrier Q3's peer-visible label needs (§2b, §2k): ≤64 characters, control characters rejected, never parsed out of `oauth-account.json`. Never logged; never written outside the lane file; never passed through PTY stdout. Regex redaction is explicitly *not* the control: `DISPATCH_CAPABILITY_PATTERN` works because dispatch capabilities carry a literal `dcap_` prefix (`runtime/orchestration/worker-transcript-payload.ts:12-13`, `:22-35`) **[code]**; OAuth tokens are opaque |
| 2 | `accounts.lane.pullRotated`, `accounts.lane.clear`, `accounts.lane.status`, **and — named in rev 22 rather than left implied by "the lane status stream" — the pair that opens and closes it, `accounts.lane.statusSubscribe` (streaming) and `accounts.lane.statusUnsubscribe`** | new-method + capability gate | `pullRotated` returns nothing when the sha matches; `statusSubscribe` is a new *method*, not a new stream **opcode**, so Rule 2 does not apply and the client capability-gates it exactly as it gates `requestSwitch`; its subscription is torn down by the one `registerSubscriptionCleanup`/`cleanupSubscriptionsForConnection` join `accounts.subscribe` already uses, and a subscriber is filed by its **grant** with the principal re-resolved on every delivery, so an unbind or a re-bind revokes the stream even though neither touches the socket. `status` carries `laneState`, the rotation receipt (cause `host` \| `cli-observed` \| `foreign-rotation`, §2c/§2e) **and the principal's published `delegatedGrantId`** — the value **every** bound desktop of that principal reads to suppress its own rotation, the named holder included (§2e), and the value the host keys `desktop_unavailable` on (§2l). Readable only by grants of that principal, through §2d's projection |
| 3 | `accounts.selectClaude` refused **for a caller that holds a lane** *and* **for any caller when the target account is lane-resident**; `removeClaude` refusal for a **resident target account**; `list`/`subscribe` per-connection projection | **Rule 3** — host-published meaning changes for old clients with no wire change | Each condition is caller- or target-scoped; none is "any lane exists". The residency edge applies to the anonymous local caller too — that is the point (§2d/§2e). With zero lanes nothing changes and the projection is byte-identical to today |
| 4 | `credentialLane` required on `TerminalCreateOptions` **and on `createManagedWorktree`'s args**, derived at every spawning RPC — `terminal.create`, `terminal.createAgentSession`/`ensureAgentSession`, `session.tabs.createTerminal`, `worktree.create`, `orchestration.workerStart` (**both** topology paths, `orchestration-worker-topology.ts:66` and `:137`), federated worker start and federated worktree create (`orchestration-federation.ts:171`, `:90`), `terminal.split` (inherit), **and `worktree.activate`, which gains `pairedDeviceId` for the sleeping-agent wake, and `terminal.recoverPane`, and `session.tabs.createTerminal`, whose lane callers are routed to the host create path, and `session.tabs.activate`, whose materialize keeps its `identity` and is gated by the adopt predicate instead (§2a, row 35)** — plus `lanePrincipalId` on the pane record, on the sleeping-agent record, on `notifier.splitTerminal` opts and on the persisted host-session binding, **carrying a principal id and never a device id** (§2a, §2h) | **Rule 3** for derivation (no new client-supplied param on any of them) | Derivation is host-observed — `ctx.pairedDeviceId` for the direct-create RPCs, and for the `inherit` edges the source pane's own `lanePrincipalId` **plus §2a's ownership predicate**; no client-supplied lane param anywhere. **The federated worktree create (`orchestration-federation.ts:90`) is the one site neither derivation reaches** — `lineage: { noParent: true }` (`:106`) leaves no source pane and its only caller identity is a home-peer fingerprint (`:63`), not a `pairedDeviceId` **[code]** — so it derives from the **link's** principal binding and **fails closed** (`terminal.lane_link_unbound`) when the link has none, which today is always (§2a). `orchestration.workerStart` and `terminal.split` newly destructure `pairedDeviceId` (`orchestration-workers.ts:29`, `terminal.ts:1276`); `RuntimeAgentSessionRpcCaller` and `worktree.activate` gain one (internal types, not wire); `session.tabs.activate` already receives it in ctx (`session-tabs.ts:41`) and merely spends it as caller identity as well as `clientNavigationId`, so that edge is not a wire change at all. The derived value is **bound to the pane**, not carried onward as a launch option — that is rev 8's correction |
| 5 | Lane `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` applied to **every** lane-pinned spawn, not only command-detected Claude launches (§2a); the whole launch **computed** for a lane per §2's closure principle — client `env`/`envToDelete`/`agentEnv` scrubbed of config-dir and auth keys, `agentArgs`/`agentCommand` allowlisted, and host-wide `settings.agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv` **ignored**; `credentialLane`/`laneState` on terminal + presence rows, **plus the additive optional `laneWipePending` of rev 16 (Rule 1 — old clients ignore it and render `absent` alone, §2f/§2h)**, enforced by one `computeLaneLaunch` at `pty.ts`'s spawn convergence (`spawnForStablePane` `:907-918` plus the ensure spawn `:4993`) so the renderer-backed create, the mobile-session create, the sleeping-agent wake and cold restore are covered on the same anchor, with the pane's lane also threaded into the provider's `buildSpawnEnv` ctx (`:2449-2492` → `local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`); `agentStatusHooksEnabled` is read lane-scoped (row 16) and an OpenClaude launch into a lane is refused (§2a) | **Rule 1** for the fields, **Rule 3** for the silent strip, the arg refusal, and the newly-armed auth-env refusal on plain-shell lane panes | An old client sending a redirecting `agentArgs`, `envToDelete: ['CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR']`, or an `env` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` on a lane pane now gets a typed refusal it has no string for; a client whose host-wide `agentDefaultArgs` silently shaped lane launches now finds they do not; a client that set `focus`/`rendererBacked` on a lane create gets a background-spawned, revealed pane instead of a renderer-created one, and a lane holder's `session.tabs.createTerminal` gets a host-minted `tabId` where it used to get the renderer's — matrix rows below. **On `win32` the lane-key *collapse* is applied post-merge inside the provider and the daemon, not at the anchor** (`local-pty-provider.ts:847+`, `daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:833`'s `collapseWindowsPathEnvKeys` precedent), because the provider rebuilds the env after `computeLaneLaunch` runs (§2m(5)); the case-insensitive **comparisons** stay at the anchor |
| 6 | Persisted state: lane id alongside each `claudeLivePtySessionIds` entry (`src/main/persistence.ts:7228-7282`) **[code]**, the per-lane watermark row, and the lane on the pane's binding row written by `persistPtyBinding` (`:6993-7027`) and on each sleeping-agent record (§2a, §2f, §2h) | local schema, not wire | Older states have no lane id → defer all accounts; no watermark → first push accepted; a pane or sleeping record with no lane is `unknown`, and split, recovery and resume of it fail closed |
| 7 | `configDir`/`sourceHome` containment refusal on `addClaudeFromConfigDir`/`addCodexFromHome`; `DeviceRegistry` `loadSucceeded` flag gating orphan reconciliation | local behavior, not wire | Both apply to the anonymous local caller too — that is the point (§2a, §2d) |
| 8 | **`principalId` on `DeviceEntry`** (`device-registry.ts:25-41`) **[code]**, minted and bound only by the host-side consent action; every lane-keyed structure re-keyed from `pairedDeviceId` to `principalId` — lane directory, ownership predicate, adopt gate, residency index, wipe predicate, presence owner label, push authorization, **the pane's persisted binding row (`lanePrincipalId`, resolved at bind time — row 4)**, the sleeping-agent record and the revoke wipe's survivor query (§2a, §2f) | local schema for the field; **Rule 3** for the meaning change | No client-supplied field anywhere: derivation is socket → `device.deviceId` (`runtime-rpc.ts:1384`) → registry row. A row with no `principalId` is a lane-less grant and behaves exactly as today. **Old-client consequence:** a person's phone and desktop now share one lane, so a switch from one moves the other's terminals — intended (Q1), and invisible on the wire |
| 9 | **`accounts.lane.requestSwitch({ delegatedAccountId })`** plus the delegable-account list on the lane status stream, and the `switch-requested` frame (§2l) | new-method (4th case) + capability gate; the frame rides the **existing** lane status stream, so no new opcode and Rule 2 does not apply | The phone never pushes: the host validates the opaque token against the principal's delegable list and asks the **desktop** to push. Refused `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` before any frame is emitted when the principal's `delegatedGrantId` holds no **lane-status subscription** — the pooled shared-control connection that subscription runs on (`ipc/runtime-environment-request-connections.ts:69`/`:122`, routed by `shouldUseSharedControlSubscription`, `ipc/runtime-environment-transport-routing.ts:294-306`), or the dedicated subscription socket it falls back to (`:246`), never a one-shot request socket (`shared/remote-runtime-client.ts:114`) **[code]**, §2l. Keyed on the designation, not on `scope`, because `DeviceScope` is `'mobile' \| 'runtime'` and `'runtime'` covers CLI grants too (`runtime-types.ts:56`, `device-registry.ts:29`) **[code]** — and not on any client-declared string, since rev 19 deletes row 12. `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out` when the subscribed desktop does not answer, and — **new in rev 23** — `accounts.lane.switch_lane_cleared` when `accounts.lane.clear` empties the lane while a request is still outstanding: that path cancels the timer, so without its own terminal frame the phone pins at `pending` with every row disabled and nothing left to time out. A push settles a request **silently** (the phone reads a `switch-failed` for a superseded id as the failure of its current one); a clear is the one lane change that must be named. Never falls back to `selectClaude` or the shared lane |
| 10 | **Per-lane usage pull (Q7)**: `getManagedUsagePanelAuthPreparation`-shaped preparation per loaded lane feeding `resolveOAuthCredentialReadOptions` (`claude-fetcher.ts:236-248`) and row 32's probe (`claude-pty.ts:247-311`) **[code]**; `paneKey` added to `ClaudeStatusLineRateLimits` (`shared/claude-statusline-rate-limits.ts:22-34`, parsed away today at `:67`/`:90`) **[code]**; `laneAccountLabel` + `laneUsage` on terminal/agent rows (§2k) | **Rule 1** for the two row fields and the local type; local behavior for the pull | The row join is `paneKey → lane → current account → usage`, not a config-dir string compare — which also removes the Windows case-folding drop (§2m(4)). **Because the paneKey now wins over the configDir, the lane usage probe must scrub inherited pane identity** the way the PTY path does (`local-pty-provider.ts:686`, keys at `shared/pane-identity-env.ts:15`), or a probe launched from an Orca terminal posts a stale `ORCA_PANE_KEY` and lands one principal's usage on another's row (§2k). The two fields differ in cross-principal visibility and the difference is deliberate: `laneAccountLabel` **is** peer-visible (Q3 is about the label the other developer sees, and §2b's `displayName` is what carries it), while a peer sees another principal's row with `laneUsage` **omitted**, never zeroed. **Gated on Windows** by `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` (§2k Fact 2, §6) |
| 12 | **`accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts({ accounts })`** — the desktop→host write of §2l step 1's delegable-account list | new-method (4th case) + capability gate | **Rev 22 adds this row, and §8 item 2 is why: rev 21 gave the list a reader and no writer at all.** §2b closes the push envelope at exactly three members and refuses a fourth `accounts.lane.push_malformed`, so the list cannot ride the push; §6's S9d tick owns only the desktop UI. Authorized **exactly as a push is** — `requireCaller` then the principal's `delegatedGrantId`, so `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated` / `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated` on the same two arms — and it writes nothing else: it never provisions a lane, never touches a credential, and never moves the designation. **Bounded before storage, the way §2b bounds its own strings:** a strict key set per entry (`clientRef`, `displayName`, `email` and nothing else), at most `MAX_LANE_DELEGABLE_ACCOUNTS` entries, each string printable and length-bounded (`displayName` ≤64 as in row 1, `email` ≤ its own bound), everything else refused `accounts.lane.delegable_list_invalid` with the previous list unchanged. The `delegatedAccountId` is **host-minted**, never the desktop's account id, and is kept stable across re-writes by the desktop's own opaque `clientRef` so a token the phone is holding stays spendable; a row the desktop stops listing loses its token and `requestSwitch` refuses it `accounts.lane.delegable_account_unknown`. **Privacy consequence, stated rather than implied:** this is the write that makes §2b's corrected sentence necessary — the host now persists the owner's **ticked** account names and opted-in emails in `claudeLaneDelegationRows` (§2b, §7 question 11). **S9d's curation tick therefore depends on S9b and not the other way round:** until the owner ticks anything the list is empty and `requestSwitch` refuses every token by name, which is the honest pre-S9d behaviour |
| 11 | **S9e (§2n)**: per-lane Linux-side config dir inside the distro, `${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-lanes/${principalId}`; `computeLaneLaunch` gains a WSL arm emitting the **Linux** path; the WSLENV invariant narrows from "no lane path" to "only a Linux lane path, under this distro's lane root", asserted in the provider's lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm where the resolved shell lives (§2a), not at `computeLaneLaunch` | local behavior, not wire | **Deferred by the owner's answer of 2026-08-22 — native shells only (§2m(8)) — so this row is a design held ready, not a scheduled delivery:** S9e is out of the S9 delivery order and out of every §6 gate, nothing in S9b–S9d waits on it, and the revival trigger is a WSL-forced pane (§2n), not a preference. Independently shippable and **not** to be landed with S9a. Presence relabels a provisioned WSL pane `credentialLane: 'grant'`; `'wsl'` survives to mean "WSL pane with no lane", so a mixed host stays legible |

**Degradation matrix.**

| Peer | Behavior |
|---|---|
| New desktop / old host | Capability absent → never pushes; keeps host-wide switching; shows the shared-host warning |
| Old desktop / new host | Never pushes → no lane loaded → its terminals launch in the shared lane as today, and its `selectClaude` is **unchanged** (it holds no lane) except when it names a lane-resident account, which is refused for everyone (§2d) |
| Mobile (either developer's) | Identified (`runtime-rpc.ts:1376-1387`) **[code]** but normally **bound to no principal**, so its terminals land in the shared lane **and it keeps host-wide account control** over every account not resident in a lane. **Once the human binds it to a principal it does not get "its own lane" and it never switches:** under Q1 a phone is bound to a *person* and shares that person's lane, and §2l establishes it can never push — it calls `accounts.lane.requestSwitch` and the desktop pushes. Rev 13's wording here ("if the human provisions it a lane, it switches in its lane instead") contradicted §2l on the client Q1 is centred on, and is corrected. On the **current** mobile client the concrete behaviour of a bound phone is a refusal: `mobile/app/h/[hostId]/accounts.tsx:228`/`:176` **[code]** sends `accounts.selectClaude` unconditionally, which §2d refuses `accounts.selection_out_of_scope` once the caller's principal holds a lane. **That is the *pre-S9b* behaviour, and S9b changes it:** §6 budgets the mobile edit that routes the switch through `accounts.lane.requestSwitch` and renders the delegable list, in a new `mobile/src/accounts/lane-delegated-switch-request.ts` plus ~40 net lines here. Rev 19 asserted `mobile/` was untouched by S9; that was wrong and rev 20 corrects it in §6 (round 8's major) |
| Second **grant**, same human (phone, tablet, second desktop) | **Q1 = YES:** bound to the same principal by the local human, so it shares **one** lane. One account, one lane, one residency — L1 is satisfied, not fought. A phone switches through §2l (it asks; the desktop pushes). A second **desktop** of the same principal reads the host-published lease and **suppresses its own token rotation** for that account; its push is additionally refused `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated` until the local human moves the lease (§2e). **The suppression does not turn on which desktop holds the lease:** every bound desktop suppresses, the `delegatedGrantId` included, because rotation of a delegated account belongs to the host alone — a rule that exempted the holder would leave the *pushing* desktop rotating the token the lane's live `claude` is holding, on the ordinary one-desktop principal (§2e). The suppression is what closes the second rotator — the refusal only keeps the watermark single-writer. A second desktop that is **unbound, never connected, or on a build with no lease code** reads no lease and is the R-DR2 row below, not this one |
| A lane whose designated pusher cannot push (a ticked phone, an `orca` CLI grant, a retired desktop) | **Inert, named and one tick from fixed.** No push arrives, no credential byte is written, `laneState` stays `absent`, launches into the lane fail closed exactly as for an unprovisioned lane, and the UI reads *"no push received from \<label\>"* beside the re-designate control (§2a). **The `requestSwitch` half of this row splits, and rev 21 stops flattening it (round 9's first major):** a ticked **phone** *is* subscribed — it already opens `accounts.subscribe` on this host (`mobile/app/h/[hostId]/accounts.tsx:122`) **[code]** — so it is **not** refused up front: the frame is emitted and the request expires `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out`. A ticked **`orca` CLI grant** or a **retired/offline desktop** holds no lane-status subscription and can hold none — the CLI has no subscribe entry point at all (`src/cli/runtime/websocket-transport.ts`, 51 lines, one-shot only; `subscribeRemoteRuntimeRequest`'s sole production caller is `ipc/runtime-environment-transport-routing.ts:246`) **[code]** — so its `requestSwitch` is refused `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` **before** any frame, exactly as for the offline-desktop row below (§2l). The host still makes no device-kind judgement anywhere in this row: both branches read whether a lane-status subscription is open for the designated grant, never what kind of device it is; the human re-ticks |
| A person's phone asking to switch while their desktop is offline | Refused `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` **before** any request frame is emitted (§2l) — "offline" means the designated grant holds no **lane-status subscription**, the pooled shared-control connection it runs on, not merely that no one-shot request socket happens to be open (rev 19). The lane's current credential is untouched, no fallback to `selectClaude`, no fallback to the shared lane. An old client renders the code plus the host's complete sentence |
| A person's phone materializing a pending tab their **desktop** minted | **Now allowed** — the adopt/materialize gate compares principals, not device ids (§2a), so the documented wake gesture (#11598) works across one person's own devices. Under rev 10 this was refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` at the person's own pane |
| Revoking **one** of a person's several grants | The grant is revoked and its sockets drop; **the lane survives**, because another grant of the principal remains bound. Only revoking the principal's **last** grant removes the lane directory and its watermark. The revoke confirmation must say which of the two it is doing (§2a, S9d). **If the revoked grant was the principal's `delegatedGrantId`, the designation is cleared with it** (rule (iii), rev 20) — the lane stays but goes silent at `laneState: 'absent'`, pushes are refused `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated`, and one re-tick on a surviving bound grant restores it; the same holds for an **unbind** |
| The person's desktop disconnects while their phone stays connected | **The lane is not wiped** — the wipe asks the socket registry whether *any* grant of the principal remains, not B2's grant-scoped `hasOtherConnections` (§2a, §2f). The phone's terminals keep launching. When the phone closes too, the lane wipes |
| A lane launch on the **Windows** host whose shell resolves to `wsl.exe` | Refused `terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported` in the provider's lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm before S9e — the one step that can observe the resolved shell (`local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`/`:733`, daemon twin `pty-subprocess.ts:723`); **not** at `computeLaneLaunch`, whose `PtySpawnOptions` carry no shell (§2a) — a Windows lane path in a WSL-branch env would create an empty config dir inside the distro and prompt for login (§2a, §2m(5)). After S9e it is served with the **Linux** lane path instead (§2n) |
| A client supplying `claude_config_dir` (any casing) on the **Windows** host | Stripped/refused: on `win32` guard 1, guard 3, `hasClaudeAuthEnvConflict` and `applyClaudeEnvPatch` compare env keys case-insensitively (§2m(5)). Before rev 11 this reached the child as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` — the §2a injection wearing different capitalization. POSIX behaviour unchanged |
| The **Orca process itself** carrying a differently-cased `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` on the **Windows** host (row 19, `runtime-paths.ts:14-25`) **[code]** | The provider's base merge reinstates that key *before* `args.env` adds the canonical one (`local-pty-provider.ts:675-676` → `git-credential-prompt-env.ts:11`), both survive, and Win32 resolves the **first case-insensitive key in block order** (`pty/windows-path-segment-merge.ts:18`, tested `windows-environment-path.test.ts:652-655`) **[code]** — i.e. the inherited shared-lane path, deterministically. Closed by moving the **collapse** post-merge into the provider and the daemon (§2m(5)), mirroring `collapseWindowsPathEnvKeys` (`daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:163-190`, called `:833`). Rev 13 sited the collapse at `computeLaneLaunch`, upstream of the merge that creates the duplicate, so this row failed silently with the pane still rendering `credentialLane: 'grant'` |
| A lane on the **Windows** host, usage bars | Statusline-derived only until the §5 Windows probe clears `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064`, which returns `null` for every per-account preparation on `win32` (§2k Fact 2). Bars cover live sessions, not idle lanes; they do not go blank |
| A **native** lane directory whose DACL could not be verified at creation | Provisioning **fails closed** — no lane exists (§2m(1)). Deliberately unlike the ordinary Windows directory-hardening path, which is async, error-swallowing and caches optimistically (`secure-file.ts:59-73`, `:201-209`) **[code]** |
| An **S9e** lane, whose root is `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…` | The §2m(1) DACL step is **not run** — `restrictWindowsPathSync` returns `false` when it cannot set one (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:60-63`) **[code]**, so rev 13's unconditional fail-closed rule would have refused **every** S9e lane. The candidate is carved out by `parseWslUncPath` (`shared/wsl-paths.ts:6-17`) exactly as §2m(4)'s UNC refusal is, and provisioning fails closed on the **in-distro `chmod 0700/0600` read-back** instead (§2n(i)) |
| A push while a live `claude` in that lane holds `.credentials.json` open (Windows) | **Usually succeeds, and fails by name when it does not:** publication goes through `renameFileWithWindowsRetry` — 6 attempts, `EPERM`/`EACCES`/`EBUSY`, ~750 ms (`codex-accounts/fs-utils.ts:200-223`), whose own comment names the Claude CLI (`:195-199`) **[code]**. The retry **rethrows** when the window is exhausted (`:220`) **[code]**, so that case is caught and refused `accounts.lane.push_write_locked` with the lane and its watermark unchanged (§2m(2)) — never an untyped throw. A bare `renameSync` (the `writeSecureFile` path, `secure-file.ts:122`) would throw, which is why the lane writer combines both primitives (§2m(2)) |
| Second Orca **install** holding the same account (laptop, reinstall, old build) — **and a second desktop of the same principal that is unbound, never connected, or on a build with no lease code**, which reads no published lease and is therefore this row and not the one above | **R-DR2, unfixable host-side (§2e):** it rotates the single-use token out from under the lane and every live terminal there fails `invalid_grant`. The host detects it (`laneState: 'reauth-required'`, `foreign-rotation` receipt) and the desktop prompts re-authentication; the operating rule is stated in the UI |
| A plain `claude` in a **non-Orca terminal on the lease-holding desktop itself** | This is R-DR2 on the machine §2e calls safe, and rev 13 covered it nowhere: `accounts.selectClaude` on that desktop materializes the account into its own `~/.claude` / `%USERPROFILE%\.claude` (`runtime-auth-service.ts:477-478`) **[code]**, which the host's residency index cannot reach and that machine's live-PTY gate cannot see. **Rule (iv) closes the Orca-reachable half** — taking the lease clears that account from the desktop's runtime file and refuses a local `selectClaude` of it (§2e). What remains is an external `claude login` or a copy placed there by something that is not Orca: detection only, `foreign-rotation` receipt, §4 residual. §7 question 9 asks whether the clear-and-refuse is the trade you want on your own machine |
| Provisioning a lane for a principal with **no designated pusher** | Refused `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated` and no lane directory is created (§2a, §2e). A lane's `delegatedGrantId` is written by a host-side consent tick over that principal's **already-bound** grants and by nothing else — rev 13's "pinned from the first accepted push" is deleted — so a provisioned lane always has a pusher, which is what lets §3 row 1's refusal be flat. **Rev 19 deletes the eligibility predicate rather than repairing it a fourth time:** any bound grant may be ticked, at any time, and the host makes no judgement about what kind of device it is — `clientCapabilities` is client-asserted, and `clientKind` is host-stored but is only the *invite's* `scope`, written at offer creation and with no desktop value (`rpc/core.ts:77` ← `runtime-rpc.ts:1784`; written `:725`, spent `:733`/`:736`; `runtime-types.ts:56`, `runtime-rpc.ts:660`) **[code]**. A phone-only principal is therefore **provisionable** and simply never loads, which is the honest rendering of "a phone can never push" (§2l). **An already-provisioned lane can also reach this state**, since unbinding or revoking the designated grant clears the designation (§2a rule (iii), rev 20): the lane survives, every `push` is refused with the same code, and the fix is the same one tick |
| A lane usage probe in flight when the principal's last connection closes | The wipe **kills the probe first and sweeps second**: mark stale, abort through `fetchViaPty`'s `options.signal` (`claude-pty.ts:221` → `settleAborted` `:335-347` → `cleanupHiddenRateLimitPty(…, { kill: true })` `:345`, `hidden-pty-cleanup.ts:25-58`) **[code]**, drop the result, then sweep (§2f, §2k). Without the fence the probe's own `claude` can rotate and write `.credentials.json` back **after** the sweep, leaving a wiped-and-reported lane holding a live credential at rest with `laneState: 'loaded'` — and §2a consequence 2's fail-closed promise false. **The kill is only half:** the lane is marked wipe-pending *before* the abort, no probe starts into a lane that is wipe-pending or `absent`, and the sweep **re-reads** the directory before reporting done (rev 15). **While that runs the row publishes `laneState: 'absent'` *and* `laneWipePending: true` (rev 16, §2f/§2h):** launches key on the first and fail closed, and nothing claims the credential is gone until the re-read is clean |
| A lane pane that is a **plain shell** | Carries the lane `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` from spawn (§2a), so typing `claude` later resolves the lane. It also has auth env vars stripped and an auth-env-defining launch refused — new behavior for such panes |
| A lane owner's `terminal.split` | Inherits the parent pane's lane. Splitting **someone else's** pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` (§2a); a build that cannot carry the lane through the renderer split path, or a pane restored without one (§2h), also fails closed |
| A lane owner's agent-session / worker / worktree-create spawn | Derives the caller's lane at the funnel; a spawner that cannot resolve one throws rather than landing in the shared lane — **including the host-internal `createManagedWorktree` path** (§2a). `orchestration.workerStart` naming **another grant's** coordinator pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned`; the same call from inside that pane (anonymous local socket) still works |
| Daemon-hosted Claude surviving an Orca restart | Startup order is observe-only sync → watermark → wipe → seed → listen (§2f); the startup pass never rotates. The reconnect push is checked against the watermark even though the lane is empty; a pre-rotation blob is refused `accounts.lane.push_stale`. The surviving session keeps running on its in-memory token |
| Corrupt/unreadable device registry at startup | Reconciliation is skipped entirely (§2a) — no lane directory is deleted, nothing is wiped that a wipe would not have wiped anyway |
| WSL target from a lane-holding grant | Falls through to today's WSL branches, labelled `credentialLane: 'wsl'` — isolated per *account*, not per grant (§2i); no lane path crosses WSLENV |
| A lane pane under **Git Bash** or **PowerShell** on the Windows host (the two shells in use, §2m(8)) | Both carry the lane `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` from the environment block, and the managed statusline is the `cmd` variant either way (`statusline-script.ts:17-18`) **[code]**, so §2k's attribution is shell-independent. Git Bash rests on MSYS passing the environment through unconverted — **[inferred]**, gated by §5 step (12c), refused `terminal.lane_shell_unsupported` if it fails. A shell **profile** (`$PROFILE`, `~/.bashrc`) can rewrite the variable after every guard: §4 residual, observable only by §5 step (0b), not closed |
| A **federated worktree create** whose link is bound to a principal / not bound | Bound: creates in the **link's principal's** lane, from the fifth consent write in `principal-registry.ts` keyed by the home-peer fingerprint (§2a, §7 q8 = (b)). Unbound: refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound`, no worktree, no terminal, no setup pane — never `{ kind: 'shared' }`. One human tick per link is what moves a link from the second row to the first |
| A federated create whose fingerprint is the **shared local-socket** one, or a re-paired link's | **Not bindable, so refused** `terminal.lane_link_unbound` (rev 16, §2a). `authenticatedCallerFingerprint` is `sha256(authToken \|\| deviceToken \|\| 'authenticated_transport')` (`orchestration-mutation-executor.ts:128-134`) **[code]** and the runtime mints **one** `authToken` for every local caller (`runtime-rpc.ts:507`, `:1664-1669`) **[code]**, so that value names no link and binding it would have created in a principal's lane for **the other developer** — the fail-open twin of the refusal. A re-pair rotates `device.token` (`device-registry.ts:97`) **[code]**, so the link's fingerprint stops matching its row and it is refused until re-ticked: one tick, and a refusal rather than a wrong-lane create in the window |
| Grant whose lane is wiped while its terminals live | Terminals keep running on the CLI's in-memory token and fail on the next refresh; presence shows `laneState: 'absent'`; new launches fail closed |
| The other developer on an old build | Terminals stay in the shared lane; they can still switch it host-wide from a local shell, moving their own and every other lane-less terminal, and by construction not any provisioned lane — and the switch is refused outright if it names a lane-resident account |
| Any old client that trips a **new** refusal | `terminal.agent_args_refused`, `terminal.lane_not_owned`, `terminal.lane_pane_unbound`, `terminal.lane_link_unbound` (§2a, the federated create), `terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported` (§2a, surfaced from inside the provider), `terminal.lane_shell_unsupported` (§2m(8), Git Bash lane panes, only if §5 step (12c) fails), `accounts.selection_out_of_scope`, `accounts.lane.push_stale`, `accounts.lane.push_malformed`, `accounts.lane.push_write_locked` (§2m(2), an exhausted Windows rename retry), `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated` (§2a/§2e — **both arms**: provisioning a principal whose bound grants include no designated pusher, *and* a `push` naming a principal that has none, which rule (iii)'s unbind/revoke clear can produce on a lane that already exists), `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated` (§2e/§3 row 1, a push from a grant that is not this principal's `delegatedGrantId`) and `accounts.lane.account_resident_elsewhere` are codes it has no string for, so it renders the raw code plus the host's `message`. **The host therefore degrades the message:** every one carries a complete human-readable sentence naming cause and remedy, and none depends on the client mapping the code. **Rev 24 — the enumeration as shipped, which is the authority (`src/shared/claude-lane-refusals.ts:7-55`) [code].** Fifty-two codes, not the sixteen this row named. Beyond those already listed: `accounts.lane.grant_unknown`, `accounts.lane.grant_not_bound`, `accounts.lane.grant_not_per_person`, `accounts.lane.grant_already_bound`, `accounts.lane.principal_unknown`, `accounts.lane.principal_id_invalid`, `accounts.lane.display_name_invalid`, `accounts.lane.not_provisioned`, `accounts.lane.caller_unidentified`, `accounts.lane.delegable_account_unknown`, `accounts.lane.delegable_list_invalid`, `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable`, `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out`, `accounts.lane.switch_lane_cleared` (rev 23), `accounts.lane.push_write_failed`, `accounts.lane.link_fingerprint_unbindable`, `accounts.lane.provision_dacl_unverified`, `accounts.lane.provisioning_platform_gated`, `accounts.lane.lane_not_owned_by_orca`, `accounts.lane.lane_path_not_contained`, `accounts.lane.lane_root_not_local`, `accounts.lane.consent_caller_not_local`, `accounts.lane.not_enabled`, `accounts.lane.delegated_elsewhere`, `accounts.lane.local_clear_locked`, `accounts.lane.clear_incomplete` (S9b round 3, §9), `terminal.lane_not_loaded`, `terminal.lane_wsl_unsupported`, `terminal.lane_agent_unsupported`, `terminal.lane_remote_pane`, `terminal.agent_env_refused`, `terminal.resume_path_refused`, `terminal.lane_unspecified`, `terminal.lane_source_unknown`, `terminal.lane_renderer_split_unsupported`, `terminal.lane_requires_workspace` and `worktree.wake_refused_not_owned`. **One code this row named was never minted:** `terminal.lane_shell_unsupported` (§2m(8), the Git Bash arm) — the shipped enumeration carries only `terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported`, because §5 step (12c) is a Windows-only live step and has not run. Rule 3 |
| A grant that sets host-wide `settings.agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv` | The setting still applies to shared-lane and non-lane launches exactly as today; it is **ignored** for every lane launch (§2 rows 13–14), so a peer cannot shape another grant's Claude invocation. A lane owner who wants per-agent default args sets them in their own lane, through their own push |
| `accounts.selectClaude` naming an account that is resident in a lane | Refused `accounts.lane.account_resident_elsewhere` for **every** caller class, including the anonymous local shell and the renderer (§2d/§2e). The local human's remedy is `accounts.lane.clear` on the holding **principal's** lane, or selecting a different account; before S9 this call always succeeded, so it is a Rule 3 meaning change |
| SSH pane whose client supplies its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` | Preserved verbatim — it names a dir on the remote host. Guard 3(a) asserts only that no **lane** path is present and that `claudeAuth` is null (§2a); no host lane is ever pinned to a `connectionId` pane, which is labelled `credentialLane: 'remote'` |
| A grant paired through the coalesced regenerate-QR link | Lane provisioning is **refused** (no `pendingExpiresAt`, §2a): its terminals stay in the shared lane and it keeps today's host-wide switching until the human re-invites that person through the named-invite flow |
| A lane holder's `terminal.create` with `focus: true` / `rendererBacked: true` | Served on the **background** branch instead of the renderer one, then published and revealed with `activate` (`orca-runtime.ts:26668-26673`) **[code]** — same pane, same focus, host-minted paneKey. With no workspace selector it is refused `terminal.lane_requires_workspace` rather than served unbound. Rule 3: an old client sees a create it did not expect to be backgrounded |
| A peer's `worktree.activate` over a lane owner's slept agents | Lane-bound records are not woken by the renderer builder: they resume only through the host create path and only for a caller the ownership predicate accepts, otherwise they stay asleep (`wake_refused_not_owned`). The peer's host-wide `agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv`/`agentCmdOverrides` reach no lane resume either way (§2a) |
| An OpenClaude launch in a lane | **Refused** (`terminal.lane_agent_unsupported`), because nothing in the tree maps `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` onto `~/.openclaude` (`hook-settings.ts:28-31`, `:71-73`) **[code]**. Typed by hand at a lane prompt it cannot be refused; the observed session downgrades the row to `credentialLane: 'shared-runtime'` and its usage is dropped rather than billed to the lane owner (§2a) |
| A lane holder's `session.tabs.createTerminal` with the desktop window open | Served by the **host** create path instead of the renderer one: host-minted `tabId`, no `terminal:requestTabCreate`, the tab revealed through publish/activate rather than at `tabCreateReply`. Rule 3 — the client gets a `tabId` it did not mint and a slightly later tab appearance; a lane-less caller is unchanged (§2a(i)) |
| A lane holder's `session.tabs.activate` on a pending tab | Materializes as today: the `identity` at `orca-runtime.ts:8181` is **kept**, so the tapped pane is reattached or respawned on its **persisted** lane (§2a(i), row 35). Another lane's pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned`; an **unbound** pane is refused `terminal.lane_pane_unbound` instead of landing on the shared `~/.claude`, so a pending tab minted before the lane existed must be re-created rather than tapped awake. A lane-less caller is unchanged |
| A `terminal.create` whose hinted `tabId`/`leafId` name a pane bound to another lane | Refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` **whatever the caller's own lane state** — a lane-less `{ kind: 'shared' }` caller may not adopt a lane-bound pane either. A dead or retired PTY under a bound paneKey respawns on the **pane's** persisted lane, for that lane's owner only; the binding row is never rewritten to `shared` (§2a(ii)) |
| A lane spawn on the **non-daemon** provider | Carries the agent-hook env even while a peer holds `agentStatusHooksEnabled: false`, because the lane now reaches `buildSpawnEnv`'s ctx (`pty.ts:2472-2473` → `local-pty-provider.ts:713`). Before rev 9 this held in daemon mode and silently failed in local mode — the lane's usage attribution with it (§2 rows 16, 30) |

## 4. Threat model

**What the design fixes.** Remote account hijack of a *lane* via `accounts.selectClaude` and deletion via `removeClaude` (today: any paired client moves or deletes everyone's credential, `accounts.ts:118-127`, `:147-151`) **[code]**; silent cross-switching between the two developers; misattribution of work — and of usage (§2k) — to the wrong account, **including on plain-shell panes, which rev 5 would have mislabelled while running on the other developer's credential (§2a)**; the process-global switch gate stalling the other developer's spawns; the `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` injection in §2a — override *and* deletion, on all three spawning RPCs, and today it reaches the real managed-account store; **cross-grant launch redirection through host-wide settings** — any paired grant can set `agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv` through an identity-ungated `settings.update` (`client-ui.ts:20-25`, `client-ui-schemas.ts:139-146`) **[code]** and shape another grant's Claude invocation, which §2's closure principle removes by computing lane launches instead of customizing them; the `--config-dir` capture path in §2d, symlinks included; the unscoped account-snapshot disclosure in `list`/`subscribe`; **the two client-reachable spawn edges rev 7's anchor missed** — a lane holder's own `focus`/`rendererBacked` create landing silently in the shared `~/.claude` through `pty:spawn`, and a peer's `worktree.activate` re-launching another grant's sleeping lane agents from host-wide settings (§2a) — and **a peer switching off `agentStatusHooksEnabled` to strip the hook coordinates from another grant's lane spawn env**, which would have ended that lane's usage attribution (row 16). **Rev 9 adds three that rev 8's anchor did not reach:** a lane holder's `session.tabs.createTerminal` landing in the shared `~/.claude` whenever a desktop window exists, because that RPC's branch key is window presence rather than a client flag (`orca-runtime.ts:27417-27418-27062`, row 29); the same `agentStatusHooksEnabled` strip surviving on the **non-daemon** provider, where the read is `pty.ts:2472-2473` inside `buildSpawnEnv` and no lane could reach it (rows 16, 30); and a client-hinted `tabId`/`leafId` adopting — or, on a dead PTY, respawning into — a pane bound to another grant's lane (`:26704-26711` before `:26739`, §2a(ii)).

**And one authority hole that is not a confidentiality concession.** Without §2a's ownership predicate, `orchestration.workerStart` (`orchestration-workers.ts:29`, pane from `params.from` at `:31`) **[code]** — on **both** its topology paths — and `terminal.split` (`terminal.ts:1276`) **[code]** would let grant B *spend* grant A's Claude account through Orca's own supported RPCs. That is categorically different from the concession below: not "B can read bytes B could read anyway", but Orca granting B the use of A's subscription.

**What it does not fix, and this is the load-bearing concession.** A paired grant is **shell-equivalent** on this host. `terminal.create` has no `clientKind` gate and its params include `command` (`terminal.ts:1007`), `env` (`:1009`) and `launchConfig.agentCommand`/`agentArgs`/`agentEnv` (`:1013-1015`), all passed straight through (`:1544-1548`) **[code]**; `terminal.split` likewise (`:1053-1054`, `:1576-1577`) **[code]**. Orca's own authorization precedent says this outright: the runtime-scope pairing "lets it create terminals and drive agents on this host (`terminal.send`, `worker-start`), so it could always post and read Run mail by driving a local pane" (`orchestration-remote-run-mailbox.ts:11-22`, esp. `:16-18`) **[code]**. Grant B can therefore call `terminal.create` with `command: cat <userData>/claude-lanes/<A>/.credentials.json` and read the output over a stream it is authorized to read. **So: lanes defend against accidental cross-switching and the `selectClaude`-class hijack; they do not defend against a hostile paired grant.**

**On the Windows Server host this whole paragraph transposes, and §2m(6) does the transposition rather than leaving it implied** — one Windows account, one `orca serve`, both developers' grants served by it; `/proc/<pid>/environ` becomes the process environment block, `ptrace` becomes `ReadProcessMemory`, the Unix socket becomes a named pipe **whose mode-bit protection is not transposed but replaced** — `createRuntimeTransportMetadata` returns `\\.\pipe\orca-${pid}-${suffix}` on `win32` (`runtime/runtime-rpc.ts:1853-1870`, endpoint `:1864`) under the comment "named pipes lack the chmod hardening of Unix sockets; a per-runtime suffix avoids a stable, guessable endpoint name" (`:1863`) **[code]** (rev 13 cited the daemon PTY-host pipe, `daemon/daemon-spawner.ts:105-108`, which is a different endpoint; corrected in §2m(6)), and reading a resident lane file at rest is *worse* than on Linux because the lane's own DACL grants FullControl to `S-1-5-32-544`, the local Administrators group (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:71`, `:85-95`) **[code]**. The alternative — two serves under two Windows users — is stated and rejected in §2m(6), because it forfeits B2 shared presence and therefore R4.

Same for the local surface on Linux: both developers run as the **same Unix user**. `0600` protects nothing from a peer who *is* that user — `cat` the lane file while it is resident, read `/proc/<pid>/environ` for the lane path, or `ptrace` a running `claude`. Root adds wiped-but-not-shredded blocks. A hostile project `.claude/settings.json` can redirect auth for any lane launched there (§2j) — a refusal, not containment. **And on the Windows host the same class arrives through the pane's own shell, which rev 15 names because the owner's answer put both shells on the daily path (§2m(8)):** PowerShell runs `$PROFILE` at session start and Git Bash runs `/etc/profile` and `~/.bash_profile`/`~/.bashrc`, all **after** `computeLaneLaunch`, after the post-merge lane-key collapse (§2m(5)) and after every assertion guard 3 makes — so one line of profile can rewrite or delete `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` inside a lane pane. It is not closable host-side and it is *inside* this paragraph's envelope rather than outside it: the profile belongs to the one Windows user both developers run as, and anyone who can edit it can equally read the resident lane file. Named, not refused — a refusal would have to police the user's own dotfiles, which §2j declines for the same reason; the only check that can see it at all is §5 step (0b), which reads the variable inside the pane after the profile has run. **Residuals accepted and named:** **OpenClaude cannot be lane-isolated today** — no `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` mapping exists for `~/.openclaude` in this tree, so lane launches of it are refused and a hand-typed session downgrades its row (§2a, unverified **[inferred]** until the §5 probe); enterprise **managed policy** outranks a lane's own settings and is invisible to §2j's refusal (root-only); the **base-URL** env class lets a lane owner redirect their own pane's CLI traffic (row 5, self-directed); **R-DR2**, a second copy of the same account rotating it out from under the lane, with only detection shipping — **rev 12 widened its membership to a second *desktop of the same principal* that is unbound, never connected, or on a build with no lease code, and rev 14 widens it again, to the *ordinary* configuration rather than only the laptop/reinstall class**: the delegating desktop's **own runtime `~/.claude` / `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`** is a third copy of the credential that §2e's rules (i)–(iii) never touched and that §2d's residency index cannot reach, because that index enumerates lanes on the *host* only (§2e). Rule (iv) closes the part Orca can reach — the lease clears that account out of the delegating desktop's runtime file and refuses a local `selectClaude` of it — and what remains is an *external* `claude login` or a non-Orca copy placed there, which is R-DR2 on the machine this design otherwise calls safe; a bound, connected, current desktop does not rotate, because it suppresses; Codex selection stays host-wide and unscoped (`accounts.ts:128-140`) **[code]**; a grant with **no** lane can still move the shared lane under other lane-less peers, for accounts not resident in a lane (§2d — Q5); WSL panes isolate per account, not per grant (§2i); lane usage is statusline-only **on Windows** until `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` is explained, and per-lane pulled elsewhere from S9b (§2k, Q7 resolved); a lane forks Claude's session history and resume surface (§2a, Q9 confirmed — intended, not a regression); an anonymous local caller can inherit any pane's lane (§2a case (ii)); and the wipe has a residency window bounded by close detection, idle timeout and the startup wipe (§2f). **Rev 11 added five, each named where it is created — and rev 12 restates the second one and moves one shape out of the "closed" column into R-DR2 above:** a **principal** is a larger blast radius than a grant — losing one of a person's devices exposes that person's lane, the owner's explicit choice under Q1 (§2a); a **phone switch requires the desktop to be reachable** and is refused by name otherwise, so the phone is not an independent client for that one operation (§2l); on Windows a **local administrator is inside every lane's ACL by construction** (§2m(6)); **WSL panes are isolated per account, not per person, until S9e ships** (§2n) — which rev 11 called the default on a Windows host and rev 15 demotes to a corner without removing, because the owner's answer is native shells (§2m(8)) and the remaining producer is a project runtime of kind `wsl`, where a lane pane is *refused* rather than silently un-isolated; and the **per-lane usage pull does not run on Windows** while `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` stands, so Windows lane usage is statusline-only (§2k). Two rev-10 residuals are now *resolved* rather than accepted: the shared lane stays open by owner decision (Q5, §2d — still a residual, now a decided one), and "a second grant of the same human cannot hold the same account" is gone, because a second grant of the same human is the same lane (Q1). The only real confidentiality fix is two Unix users, which the owner has ruled out.

If the owner wanted R1 to hold against a hostile grant, the achievable control would be **lane-strict mode** — and **Q6 is answered: OFF, it is not built.** §2's closure principle already removes the *customization* half — `env`, `agentEnv`, `agentCommand`/`agentArgs` and resume paths are computed or allowlisted for a lane launch — so what lane-strict adds is the rest: once the caller holds a lane, its sockets may not supply a free-form `command` at all, only named launch configs the host already knows. A large functional cut, and the owner has declined it, so **R1 is an authority guarantee and not a confidentiality one** — stated here once more because it is the single most important limit in this document and the decision has now made it permanent for S9.

**Version pin, and what happens if it fails.** The design rests on one behavior — the running CLI re-reads relocated credentials — and credential resolution has changed across releases (the v2.1.225 note) **[doc]**; Orca already pins its OAuth constants against a specific binary ("verified against the installed `claude` binary (2.1.177)", `claude-accounts/oauth-refresh.ts:4-8`) **[code]**. Record the tested range here, probe `claude --version` at lane provisioning, and refuse outside it — on macOS unconditionally (§2i). **If the §5 live probe fails on the pinned version, S9 does not die; R2 degrades.** Lanes still isolate: R1, R3 and R4 hold, because isolation depends on the *launch-time* `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, not on re-reads. What is lost is live switching — a push then takes effect for **new** terminals in that lane only, `laneState` gains `'restart-required'`, and the AccountsPane hedge (`AccountsPane.tsx:808`) **[code]** becomes the accurate UI instead of a hedge.

## 5. Test plan

Vitest under `config/vitest.config.ts`. Each new guard gets a negative control; each authorization check gets a mutation proof (delete the check → a named test must fail).

- **S9a lanes + launch** — `principal-credential-lane.test.ts`: symlink, escape, foreign-marker and wrong-segment rejections; a 36-character non-UUID lane id refused **at creation**; lane `settings.json` carries the managed hook block plus exactly the §2a mirror allowlist — asserted item by item: `permissions.allow/deny/ask/additionalDirectories` and `model`/`outputStyle` present, `permissions.defaultMode`, user `hooks`, `env`, `apiKeyHelper` and `awsAuthRefresh`/`awsCredentialExport` **absent** (mutation proof: mirroring the host's `env` or `apiKeyHelper` turns a named test red); a fresh lane's `.claude.json` carries the onboarding allowlist and the mirrored `mcpServers` **minus** any entry whose `env` names `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or an auth var, and **no** project-trust map and **no** project `history`; the lane's mirrored `agents/`, `commands/`, `skills/` and `output-styles/` are present item by item (**mutation proof:** dropping any one turns a named test red); a lane's transcripts are its own and pre-S9 resume paths are refused (§2a/§2g); a `push` from a grant bound to no principal, and a `push` naming an **unprovisioned** principal, are each refused and provision nothing (**mutation proof:** auto-provisioning on push turns it red); provisioning a device entry with **no `pendingExpiresAt`** is refused while a minted row provisions (**setup, corrected in rev 19:** the provisioning row needs a **designation** and nothing else — a bound grant the human has ticked, connected or not — so the `pendingExpiresAt` discriminator is reached on its own terms rather than being shadowed by an eligibility refusal that would have made the arm pass for the wrong reason, §2a; **mutation proof:** dropping the discriminator turns it red); startup reconciliation deletes an orphan lane dir, and **a registry whose `load` threw deletes nothing** (**mutation proof:** removing the `loadSucceeded` gate turns it red and wipes every lane); a planted `.credentials.json.<pid>.<uuid>.tmp` is removed by both the wipe and the next lane open (**mutation proof:** a filename-scoped wipe turns it red). `runtime-auth-service.test.ts` (FULL sweep, 3932 lines): the lane branch returns the lane `envPatch`, `stripAuthEnv: true`, `managedRefreshDeferredByLivePty`, and a `provenance` that is `lane:`-prefixed, contains no `pairedDeviceId`, and fails `isManagedClaudeAuth`; a WSL target from a lane-holding grant returns the WSL branch; host branch unchanged. `ipc/pty.test.ts` (FULL sweep): two creates on two grants get two different `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` values; an anonymous create gets none; an SSH pane (`connectionId` set) gets **no** lane path **and keeps its own client-supplied `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` verbatim** — the guard 3(a)/3(b) split (**mutation proofs:** deleting the `connectionId` condition from the lane resolver turns the first red; making guard 3 delete client config dirs unconditionally turns the second red); a spawn env taking the WSL branch never contains a lane path (`local-pty-provider.ts:762-764`, `pty-subprocess.ts:762-764`); a split of a lane-owned pane inherits that lane. **Command-independence — the §2a decoupling, its own block:** a lane-pinned `terminal.create` with **no `command`**, a worktree **default tab**, a `session.tabs.createTerminal` plain shell, and a **non-Claude `startupAgent`** each carry the lane `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, are registered in the lane's live-PTY set, and refuse an auth-env-defining `env` (**mutation proof:** re-gating the lane branch on `isClaudeLaunchCommand` (`pty.ts:1924-1931`) must turn *all four* red). **Closure principle — host-wide settings must not shape a lane launch:** with `settings.update({ agentDefaultArgs: { claude: '--settings /tmp/b.json' }, agentDefaultEnv: { claude: { ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: 'x' } } })` applied by a *second* grant, a lane launch on the first grant reaches the PTY (path A, `pty.ts:5019`/`:5066`) with neither the flag nor the env — asserted for a bare `command: 'claude'` in a repo-backed workspace **and** for a `startupAgent` create, the two branches of `resolveAgentTerminalCreateOptions` (`:26096` and `:26109`). **Mutation proofs:** computing the lane launch *before* `resolveAgentTerminalCreateOptions` (rev 6's placement) or at `ptyController.spawn` in `orca-runtime.ts` (rev 7's) must turn both red — the second because the `agentTeamsPlan` re-derivation sits between that anchor and the spawn. A shared-lane launch with the same settings still receives them. **Split and renderer edges:** the same computation covers `splitPtyBackedTerminal` and `pty:spawn` because it lives in `pty.ts` (**mutation proof:** moving it back into `createTerminal` turns the split `env`-vector test *and* the renderer-backed create test red). **Funnel coverage — one test per spawning RPC, all asserting the caller's lane and not the shared one:** `terminal.create`, `terminal.createAgentSession`/`ensureAgentSession` (proving the new `pairedDeviceId` is read, not the collapsed `clientId`, **and** that the ensure branch's direct `provider.spawn` at `pty.ts:5019` gets the lane), `session.tabs.createTerminal` (both with and without an authoritative window), `worktree.create` (default-tab, startup and setup panes), `orchestration.workerStart` for `worktree: 'current'` (`:65`) **and for `'new-child'`/`'new-top-level'` (`:145` → `createManagedWorktree`, whose worker terminal and setup pane must land in the coordinator's lane, not shared — mutation proof: dropping `credentialLane` from `createManagedWorktree`'s args turns it red)**, federated worker start, the coordinator loop, and pane recovery. **Mutation proof for the funnel:** making `credentialLane` optional with a `'shared'` default must turn *every* one red; and a host-internal caller passing neither a lane nor explicit `shared` must throw. **Ownership predicate:** grant B calling `orchestration.workerStart` with `params.from` naming grant A's coordinator pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` on both topology paths and creates no worker terminal, worktree or setup pane; B splitting A's pane is refused, **and so is B reaching the same split through `agentTeams.tmuxCompat`** (mutation proof: placing the predicate in the `terminal.split` handler instead of `splitPtyBackedTerminal` turns the tmuxCompat test red while the split test stays green); the same `workerStart` from an anonymous local socket still inherits; **a federated worktree create whose link *is* bound creates in the link's principal's lane, and one whose link is unbound is refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound` and creates no worktree, no terminal and no setup pane** — rev 15's §7-question-8 answer, asserted on both arms (**mutation proofs:** falling through to `{ kind: 'shared' }` turns the unbound arm red; binding `{ kind: 'grant' }` as the lane **value** turns the bound arm red, because the `credentialLane` union has no grant case — rev 18's restatement, since the link **row** is grant-keyed *by design* (`{ homePeerFingerprint, boundDeviceId, boundAt }`, the create reading `principalOf(boundDeviceId)`, §2a/§6/§7 q8), so the red arm is the lane value and never the row's key). **And the binding's *key* rule, its own three assertions — rev 16's blocker fix, which is the fail-open direction and therefore needs its own fixtures:** a bind attempt on the fingerprint of the runtime's **shared** `authToken` (`runtime-rpc.ts:507`) is **refused as unbindable**, and a `federationAttachStart` arriving over the local socket therefore still gets `terminal.lane_link_unbound` (**mutation proof:** accepting any fingerprint the consent surface is handed turns it red, in a fixture where developer B's local `orca` call creates a worktree, a startup terminal and a setup pane in developer A's lane — the fail-open twin of the refusal); the same for the `sha256('authenticated_transport')` fallback value (`orchestration-mutation-executor.ts:132`); a fingerprint matching **zero** or **two** registry rows' `token` is not offered and not bindable, while one matching exactly one row binds and records `boundDeviceId` (**mutation proof:** dropping the one-row requirement turns the zero-match case red); and a **re-paired** grant's link — its `token` rotated at `device-registry.ts:97` — no longer matches its stored row and its create is refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound` rather than served on the stale binding (**mutation proof:** storing only the fingerprint with no `boundDeviceId`/token check leaves the stale binding live and turns it red); a restored pane with no lane id refuses both split and recovery. **Mutation proof:** deleting the predicate must turn the workerStart and split tests red. **Env/arg injection — four vectors × three spawning RPCs.** `env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `launchConfig.agentEnv.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `envToDelete: ['CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR']` and a settings-redirecting `agentArgs`, each through `terminal.create`, `session.tabs.createTerminal` and `worktree.create`, plus the `env` vector via `terminal.split`. The `envToDelete` case asserts the *resulting spawn env* on **both** paths, after `:4839` and after `:6498`. With `computeLaneLaunch` deleted, guard 3(b) must still refuse all of them — **including in the zero-lane state**. Containment: `ompResumeFilePath` and `resumeProviderSession.transcriptPath` pointing into another lane, or into the shared `~/.claude`, are refused on all three RPCs. **Post-anchor invariant — the rev-9 anchor, its own block:** a lane spawn's *provider* env (the object `LocalPtyProvider` hands the process, after `buildSpawnEnv` at `local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`, after the `envToDelete` replay at `:725-726` and after `promoteAgentTeamsShimPath` at `:851`) carries exactly the lane's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and none of `CLAUDE_AUTH_ENV_VARS` — asserted on the object itself, not on the host env (**mutation proofs:** letting `spawnOptions.envToDelete` keep `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` past `computeLaneLaunch` turns it red, because `:725-726` then deletes the lane; anchoring `computeLaneLaunch` at the assembly points instead of `spawnForStablePane` turns the `buildSpawnEnv` hook assertion red); and the agent-session-ensure spawn (`pty.ts:5019`) is covered too — a lane `terminal.createAgentSession` reaching `provider.spawn` **directly** must carry the lane env (**mutation proof:** applying the anchor only inside `spawnForStablePane` turns this one red, and *only* this one). **Pane-record binding — its own block:** a lane `terminal.create` with `focus: true` and with `rendererBacked: true` is served on the background branch and its pane carries the lane (**mutation proof:** honoring either flag for a lane create, i.e. letting it reach `terminal:requestTabCreate`, turns it red); a lane create with no workspace selector is refused rather than served unbound; a `pty:spawn` on a lane pane whose `args` carry `env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, a settings-redirecting `launchConfig.agentArgs` **and a forged lane-shaped field** spawns with the pane's own lane and none of the three (**mutation proof:** reading the lane from `args` instead of the pane record turns it red); a client-hinted `tabId`/`leafId` naming a pane bound to another lane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` **for every caller class** — asserted once for a lane caller and once for a lane-**less** `{ kind: 'shared' }` caller, and asserted again with the target pane's PTY **dead**, where the respawn into that paneKey must run on the pane's persisted lane for its owner and be refused for anyone else, with the binding row unchanged (**mutation proof:** rewriting a bound row to `shared` on a refused adopt, or gating the check on the caller holding a lane, turns these red); a lane `session.tabs.createTerminal` **with an authoritative window present** is served by `createRuntimeOwnedMobileSessionTerminal`, its pane is host-minted and lane-bound, and no `terminal:requestTabCreate` is dispatched (**mutation proof:** restoring the window-presence branch for a lane caller — or letting the renderer-minted `identity` through at `orca-runtime.ts:27545` — turns it red), while the same call from a lane-less caller still takes the renderer branch; **Activate/materialize — its own controls, because the drop must *not* reach this edge:** a lane holder taps a **pending** tab of their own lane pane and `session.tabs.activate` materializes it — the pane is reattached (or respawned into the same paneKey when its PTY is dead) under the *same* `tabId`/`leafId`, the tab reaches `status: 'ready'`, and the worktree gains **no** second tab (**mutation proof:** dropping `identity` at `orca-runtime.ts:8181` the way the create call sites drop it turns this named test red on both assertions — the tapped tab stays non-ready and a second, host-minted tab appears); grant B tapping a pending tab of grant A's lane-bound pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` and spawns nothing (**mutation proof:** removing the `:26704-26711` predicate turns this cross-lane materialize test red — it is the *only* gate on this edge); a lane holder tapping an **unbound** pending tab is refused `terminal.lane_pane_unbound` and no PTY is created on the shared config dir (**mutation proof:** falling through to `shared` turns it red); and a lane-**less** caller's materialize behaves exactly as today, identity intact; the lane survives a runtime restart on the persisted binding row — asserted across **all four** `persistPtyBinding` writers, including the two reattach writers (`pty.ts:820`, `ssh-relay-session.ts:2558`), whose rewrite must preserve the lane (**mutation proof:** re-emitting a row without the lane on reattach turns it red) — and a restored pre-lane pane refuses split, recovery and resume; `terminal.recoverPane` on a relay-lease pane stays `credentialLane: 'remote'` and never adopts a lane. **Wake and resume:** grant B's `worktree.activate` over grant A's slept lane agent leaves it asleep (`wake_refused_not_owned`) and creates no pane; A's own activate resumes it in A's lane, with B's `settings.update({ agentDefaultArgs, agentDefaultEnv })` and `agentCmdOverrides` absent from the spawn (**mutation proof:** waking a lane record through the renderer builder turns both red); a cold-restore respawn of a lane pane keeps the lane and drops the renderer's settings-derived args. **Agent Teams:** a lane launch whose `agentTeamsPlan` rebuilds `command`/`env`/`envToDelete` still spawns with the lane env intact (**mutation proof:** anchoring `computeLaneLaunch` before the plan, i.e. rev 7's placement, turns it red). **Hooks:** with a peer's `settings.update({ agentStatusHooksEnabled: false })` applied, a lane spawn still carries the agent-hook env (`pty.ts:1770`) while a shared-lane spawn does not — asserted **twice, once per provider mode**: on the daemon path (`pty.ts:4730`/`:6455`) and on the LocalPtyProvider path, where the read is `:2478` inside `buildSpawnEnv` (**mutation proofs:** a host-wide read for lane panes turns both red; dropping the lane from the `buildSpawnEnv` ctx turns *only* the local-mode one red — the round-8 MAJOR A regression). Same shape for `networkProxySettings` (row 28/30): a lane pane's proxy settings come from the host, and the assertion is that nothing lane-scoped depends on them. **OpenClaude:** a lane `startupAgent`/command naming OpenClaude is refused `terminal.lane_agent_unsupported`, and a hook-observed OpenClaude session in a lane pane flips its row to `shared-runtime` and drops its usage instead of attributing it. `keychain.test.ts` (macOS reader, runs on every platform over a faked backend): a lane read/write resolves the **scoped** service only (**mutation proof:** restoring the `[scoped, unsuffixed]` walk of `:108-113` on the lane branch turns it red). `claude-accounts/service.test.ts`: `addClaudeFromConfigDir`/`addCodexFromHome` refuse a path under `claude-lanes`, **including from an anonymous local caller** and **including a symlink whose realpath lands inside** (mutation proof: a `resolve`-only `startsWith` passes the symlink and turns it red).
- **S9b push + scoping + refresh + attribution** — real-socket integration following `src/main/runtime/multi-client-navigation-isolation.integration.test.ts` (two E2EE-paired clients over real `ws`) **[code]**: A pushes → A's lane file changes, B's lane and the shared lane do not; B cannot target A's lane (no param); B's `selectClaude` is refused **only when B holds a lane**, and an unprovisioned B's still works (mutation proof: "any lane exists" turns it red); **and, on the other edge, `selectClaude` naming an account resident in A's lane is refused for B, for an unprovisioned grant, for the renderer and for the anonymous local socket alike** (mutation proof: dropping `assertNotLaneResident` from `selectClaude` while leaving it on `removeClaude` turns a named test red), while an unrelated account still selects; a shared-lane `claude login` performed between two syncs is caught because the `host` row is re-derived on each sync and before each residency check (mutation proof: deriving it only at startup turns it red); `removeClaude` refused for a resident account, allowed otherwise (mutation proof: host-scoping that condition turns a named test red); `accounts.list` **and both `subscribe` emit points** (`accounts.ts:194-196`, `:221`) to B never contain A's email or usage; an anonymous local socket keeps host-wide selection. **Mutation proof:** replacing `ctx.pairedDeviceId` with a params-derived id turns a named test red. **Push validation:** an oversized member, an extra member, a `.credentials.json` failing the `claudeAiOauth.accessToken` shape (`runtime-auth-service.ts:927-929`), and a non-object `oauth-account.json` are each refused `accounts.lane.push_malformed` **and write nothing to disk**; so are an over-long (>64 char) and a control-character `displayName`, and a push from a grant that is not the principal's `delegatedGrantId` is refused `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated` and writes nothing. **Peer-visible label (Q3):** grant B reading a terminal row of principal A's lane sees A's principal label **and** the account's owner-set `displayName`, and does **not** see the email, the account id or `laneUsage` (mutation proof: sourcing that label from the pushed `oauth-account.json`'s `emailAddress` instead of the envelope's `displayName` turns a named test red). **Watermark:** two consecutive switches from the same desktop both succeed (**mutation proof:** dropping the push-arm watermark write turns the *second* push red with `push_stale` — the rev-5 bug); a stale-sha push is rejected; an older-issued-at push is rejected even with `reauthenticated: true`; a lane rotated out-of-band by its own CLI produces a `cli-observed` receipt on the next launch tick, moves the watermark, and a replay of the pre-rotation push is refused.
