Refresh + residency: a live PTY in the lane holding account X defers **X's** rotation and not another account's; a push of an account already resident in another lane is refused; **a push of the account whose refresh token is live in an *unmanaged* `~/.claude` is refused via the sha-keyed host row** (mutation proof: seeding the host row from settings turns it red); a delegated account on the **lease-holding** desktop — the `delegatedGrantId` itself — with an expiring token and no local PTY does not call `refreshManagedAccountTokenIfNeeded`, **and still does not after a simulated desktop restart** (**mutation proof:** exempting the named `delegatedGrantId` from suppression turns this red, which is the rev-12 blocker: it is the *pushing* desktop, so an exemption re-opens double rotation on the ordinary one-desktop-plus-phone principal); a simulated *foreign* rotation (token changed, refresh fails) yields `laneState: 'reauth-required'` and a `foreign-rotation` receipt rather than a silent failure (R-DR2's detection path); a slow push in lane A does not delay lane B's write. Attribution: a statusline post carrying lane A's configDir lands on lane A's usage row and not the shared bar; an unknown configDir is still dropped (`rate-limits/claude-usage-attribution.ts:105-125`); a published `usageMetadata.authProvenance` for a lane contains no `pairedDeviceId`.
- **S9c lifecycle + gate** — **a wipe whose probe cannot be confirmed dead inside the bounded window publishes `laneState: 'absent'` *and* `laneWipePending: true`, retries the sweep, and reports the wipe done only after a clean post-sweep re-read (rev 16, §2f/§2h)** (**mutation proof:** clearing `laneWipePending` at the mark-and-abort step instead of at the clean read-back turns it red, because the row then claims the credential is gone while a full blob can still be at rest); last-connection close wipes only that lane, and the predicate is the **principal's**, not the grant's: the site reads `socket.device.deviceId` (`runtime-rpc.ts:1384`) **[code]**, never `deviceToken`, and resolves it through `principalOf` before asking whether any authenticated socket of that principal remains (§2f). Two sockets on one grant: closing the first wipes nothing; two sockets of one principal on *different* grants: same answer, asserted in the principal-binding arm below. (Rev 13 stated this bullet in rev 10's grant-keyed form — "keyed by `deviceId` not `deviceToken`" — which is the first hop only; §2f is the normative home and it re-keys to the principal.) Idle-timeout close wipes; **startup runs observe-only sync → watermark → wipe → seed → listen** (simulate a crash by leaving credential files across a runtime restart — mutation proofs: deleting the startup wipe turns one test red; taking the watermark *after* the wipe, or exempting empty lanes from the freshness check, turns the daemon-survival test red); **a startup `syncLane` over a lane whose token is expiring performs no rotation** — no call to `refreshClaudeOauthCredentials` before the seed (mutation proof: letting trigger (4) rotate turns it red); the lane gate: a spawn in lane A during a push to lane A is refused — asserted once for a `claude` command and once for a **plain shell**, per §2a — and a spawn in lane B is not (mutation proof: dropping `begin/endClaudeAuthSwitch(laneId)` from the push handler turns it red); reconnect + re-push restores; **revoking a principal's *last* grant removes the directory and the watermark, while revoking one of two grants bound to the same principal removes neither** — the same assertion the S9e arm makes, stated identically in both places (**mutation proof:** keying the revoke wipe on the grant, or hooking it beside `forgetGrant` *after* `removeDevice` has already dropped the row, turns the two-grant case red); a launch into an unloaded lane fails closed — **including a plain-shell lane pane, which must fail closed for the same reason** (§2a consequence 2); **and the close-wipe fences an in-flight usage probe (§2f, §2k):** with a probe running in lane A, the principal's last socket closes, the probe's `claude` is **killed** through the abort handle (`claude-pty.ts:221` → `settleAborted` `:335-347` → `cleanupHiddenRateLimitPty(term, termDisposables, { kill: true })` `:345`) **[code]** *before* the sweep, its result is dropped, and after the sweep the lane holds no `.credentials.json` and `laneState` reads `absent`; **a probe requested while that wipe is pending is refused rather than started, and the sweep's own post-sweep re-read catches one that was not** (**mutation proof:** dropping the re-read and admitting a probe mid-wipe leaves a credential at rest with the wipe still reported done, §2f) (**mutation proofs:** sweeping without killing turns it red — a scripted post-sweep write by the probe's CLI leaves a credential at rest and the lane reading `loaded`; and disposing only the `registerHiddenRateLimitPty` registration, which deletes a Set entry and kills nothing (`hidden-pty-cleanup.ts:15-18`) **[code]**, turns a second one red the same way). `live-pty-gate.test.ts` (FULL sweep): a *host* switch in lane A does not throw for a spawn in lane B; seeded-but-unattributed session ids defer every account until `confirmSeededClaudeLivePtys` runs.
- **Principal binding + phone switch (S9a and S9b arms; not the S9e slice, which is §2n's WSL lane)** — `principal-binding.test.ts`: a lane is provisioned for a **principal**, and two grants bound to it resolve the *same* lane directory (mutation proof: keying the lane by `pairedDeviceId` turns it red); a grant bound to no principal addresses no lane and its `accounts.lane.push` is refused `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated` (it is no principal's `delegatedGrantId`), while a `push` naming a principal that has **no designation** — an unprovisioned one, or one whose designated grant was unbound or revoked under §2a rule (iii) — is refused `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated`, the push arm of the provisioning code (rev 20 names both codes here; round 8's sixth minor); a device row with **no `pendingExpiresAt`** cannot be bound to a principal at all (mutation proof: dropping the discriminator turns it red); the `--pair-name` string is **not** consulted anywhere in the binding path (mutation proof: resolving a principal by matching `DeviceEntry.name` turns a named test red — two rows both named `Ana` must resolve to two principals). **Adopt/materialize across one person's devices:** the phone materializes a pending tab the desktop minted and it succeeds; the *other* principal's is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned`; an unbound pane is still refused `terminal.lane_pane_unbound`. **Wipe predicate:** two sockets of one principal on *different* grants — closing the desktop's wipes **nothing**, closing the phone's wipes the lane (mutation proof: reusing B2's `hasOtherConnections`, which compares `device.deviceToken` at `mobile-socket-wiring.ts:218`, turns the first assertion red); revoking one of two grants leaves the lane, revoking the last removes it and its watermark. **Phone switch (§2l):** `accounts.lane.requestSwitch` with a valid `delegatedAccountId` emits a `switch-requested` frame and returns `pending` and **writes no credential**; with the desktop's socket closed it is refused `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` **and emits no frame** (mutation proof: emitting the frame first, then refusing, turns it red); an unknown or another principal's `delegatedAccountId` is refused; an unanswered request expires `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out` with the lane byte-identical; a second **desktop** grant of the same principal pushing without the lease is refused `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated`, **and — the assertion that actually closes the second rotator — a bound desktop that reads a published delegation performs no `refreshManagedAccountTokenIfNeeded` for that account, asserted twice: once where the `delegatedGrantId` names *someone else* and once where it names *this desktop*, before *and* after a simulated restart and while disconnected** (mutation proof: keying suppression on the arrival of a push, i.e. the refusal alone, turns it red — the rotation happens with no push in sight; a second mutation, un-suppressing on disconnect, turns the disconnected assertion red). **Bind, unbind, audit (§2a):** binding onto an already-bound row is refused, re-binding is unbind-then-bind, and all three write an audit row. **The consent-disambiguation refusal rev 13 asserted here is removed with the rule it tested (§2a, round 3's first major).** It was written over a *different* fact set than the normative text stated, and the normative rule could not fire at all: `deviceId` is `randomUUID()` (`device-registry.ts:95`) and `pairedAt` is `Date.now()` (`:99`) **[code]**, so two rows are never indistinguishable on all of them. What is asserted instead is what actually holds: the **binding** surface is reachable **only** at mint and at that grant's named invite, where the host already knows the row it is binding (rev 19's rule (i)), while the **designation** surface is a separate act over already-bound rows and is deliberately reachable afterwards — and a **mis-tick is recovered, not prevented**: unbind restores the unbound state, the audit row records device/principal/direction/timestamp, and no live pane re-points, because panes persist `lanePrincipalId` and never a device id. The `--pair-name` assertion below is unchanged and is the one that keeps the label out of the binding path. **Lease bootstrap (§2a, §2e — round 3's first blocker):** provisioning a lane **also** designates its `delegatedGrantId`, so a lane is never created without one and §3 row 1's flat refusal is satisfiable (the only later way to hold none is rule (iii)'s clear, asserted below and refused `no_pusher_designated` rather than carved out); a principal **none of whose bound grants has been designated** is refused `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated` and no lane directory is created (**mutation proofs:** letting the first accepted push pin the lease — rev 13's trap-door — turns a named test red, in which a *second* grant of the principal races the designated one and seizes the lease with a push; and provisioning without designating a pusher turns a second red, in which the lane exists, every push is refused `push_not_delegated`, and `laneState` never leaves `absent`). **And the designation has its own fixtures — rev 19, replacing every eligibility-predicate arm revs 16–18 accumulated (`scope: 'runtime'`, the host-advertised capability, the client-declared `accounts.lane.push.v1`, and the `declaredPushers` memo), all of which are deleted with the mechanism they tested.** **The surface:** a designation row is offered for **every** grant bound to the principal and for no grant bound to another, and the fixture designates a grant whose handshake carries only the two-element list every client sends today (`shared/remote-runtime-client.ts:140`) **[code]** and expects it to **succeed** (**mutation proof:** filtering the list on `scope: 'runtime'`, on `ctx.clientCapabilities`, or on any `clientKind` value turns it red — the host makes no device-kind judgement, §2a). **Timing:** the identical tick succeeds at provisioning time, an hour later on an already-provisioned lane, and after a runtime restart with no reconnection in between (**mutation proof:** confining the designation surface to mint or to that grant's first connect — revs 17–18's framing — turns all three red). **Persistence:** a designated principal survives a runtime restart, a socket close and a disconnect, and the first push after any of them is still checked against the stored `delegatedGrantId` (**mutation proof:** holding the designation in a process-lifetime in-memory set — rev 18's `declaredPushers` carrier — turns it red on the restart arm). **Audit:** designate and re-designate each write a row carrying the designated grant, the principal, what happened and a timestamp, and **no direction** (§2a rule (iii)). **Clearing — rev 20, round 8's fourth minor:** unbinding the designated grant, and separately revoking it, each leave the principal with **no designation** rather than a `delegatedGrantId` naming a deleted row; on the two-grant case §2f keeps alive (desktop revoked, phone bound and connected) the **lane survives**, `laneState` is `absent`, the next `push` is refused `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated`, the surface renders an unticked list rather than a label, and one re-tick on the surviving grant restores the lane (**mutation proof:** leaving the stale id in place turns it red two ways — the push is refused `push_not_delegated` instead, and the *"no push received from \<label\>"* string has no `DeviceEntry` to source rule (ii)'s label from). The clear writes its own audit row. **And the never-connected arm inverts, which is the clearest single marker that the predicate is gone:** a bound grant with `lastSeenAt === 0` (`isMintedPendingDevice`, `device-registry-pending-grants.ts:14-16`) **[code]** **is** offered a designation row and the lane provisions, loading on that grant's first push (**mutation proof:** refusing the row for a `lastSeenAt === 0` grant turns it red — that is revs 17–18's first-connect-only consequence rendered as the test it used to be). **Mis-designation, asserted as recoverable and never as prevented:** a **phone** grant designated as the pusher provisions the lane; the lane then sits at `laneState: 'absent'` with **no credential byte written** and every launch into it failing closed; its `requestSwitch` satisfies §2l's liveness precondition — the phone holds a lane-status subscription like any other grant — so the frame is emitted and the request expires `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out` with the lane byte-identical; and **re-designating a real desktop makes the next request succeed** and the lane load (**mutation proof:** asserting instead that a phone is refused *at designation* turns it red; the control is the human tick plus re-designation, §2a, and the suite must not encode a guarantee the host cannot make). **An `orca` CLI grant is the *opposite* arm rather than the same three assertions, and rev 21 corrects it (round 9's first major):** a designated CLI grant provisions the lane and leaves it at `laneState: 'absent'` with no credential byte written in the same way, but its `requestSwitch` never reaches a frame — it is refused `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` with **no frame emitted**, because it holds no lane-status subscription and the client has no way to open one (`src/cli/runtime/websocket-transport.ts` is one-shot only; `subscribeRemoteRuntimeRequest`'s sole production caller is `ipc/runtime-environment-transport-routing.ts:246`) **[code]**; a **retired/offline desktop** asserts identically (**mutation proof:** asserting `switch_timed_out` for either — rev 20's wording — turns it red, and that arm could only ever pass against a faked subscription no CLI client can open). Re-designating a real desktop makes the next request succeed here too. **The liveness precondition's socket, rev 19 against round 7 (§2l):** `requestSwitch` is refused `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` with **no frame emitted** when the designated grant holds only a one-shot request socket, and is **accepted** when it holds the pooled shared-control connection carrying its lane-status subscription (**mutation proof:** resolving the precondition over any authenticated socket at all — including the one-shot `sendRemoteRuntimeRequestOnSocket` handshake at `shared/remote-runtime-client.ts:114`/`:140` **[code]** — turns the first arm red; keying it on `scope` turns it red a second time, since a connected `orca` CLI grant would then pass). **Lease rule (iv), the delegating desktop's own runtime file (§2e — round 3's fourth major):** with account X delegated, that desktop's runtime `.credentials.json` no longer materializes X — a `selectClaude(X)` on the lease-holding desktop is refused locally, and an X already materialized there when the lease is taken is cleared (**mutation proof:** governing only `refreshManagedAccountTokenIfNeeded` and managed launches, i.e. rev 13's rules (i)–(ii) alone, turns it red: a plain `claude` in a non-Orca terminal on that desktop reads the file and rotates the lane's token). **Pane binding is a principal id:** a pane created by grant G of principal P still resolves P's lane after G is revoked — `deviceRegistry.removeDevice` at `runtime-rpc.ts:648`/`:660` drops the row — and the pane renders `credentialLane: 'grant'`, not `'unknown'`, and still splits, recovers and resumes (mutation proof: persisting the grant id and resolving `principalOf` at read time turns all four red — **in that mutated build** unbinding G re-points the pane at nothing at all, which is the observable the proof keys on). **On the shipped design unbind re-points no live pane at all**, and the suite asserts that positively as well: unbind G, and P's panes still resolve P's lane (§2a rule (iii)). *(Rev 20 moves the mutated-build clause inside the parenthetical; standing outside it, it read as a negation of rule (iii) it now abuts — round 8's seventh minor.)* **The mobile client, rev 20 (§6's S9b, round 8's major):** the phone-side arm is asserted against the **real** module rather than a synthetic client — `mobile/src/accounts/lane-delegated-switch-request.ts` under `mobile`'s own **Vitest** suite (`mobile/package.json`'s `test` script is `vitest run`; `vitest` is its devDependency) beside `mobile/src/accounts/mobile-accounts-route.test.ts` **[code]**: with the caller's principal holding a lane the switch call is `accounts.lane.requestSwitch` and **never** `accounts.selectClaude`, with no lane it stays `accounts.selectClaude` (mutation proof: routing unconditionally to `requestSwitch` turns the lane-less arm red, and routing unconditionally to `selectClaude` — today's behaviour at `mobile/app/h/[hostId]/accounts.tsx:228`/`:176` **[code]** — turns the lane arm red with the `accounts.selection_out_of_scope` refusal §3's Mobile row describes); `pending` renders as pending and resolves only from the lane status stream, never from the return value; and `desktop_unavailable`/`switch_timed_out` render the **host's** `message` verbatim, with no client-side code table (mutation proof: mapping the codes client-side turns it red — §3's Rule-3 row is what makes the sentence the host's job). **Delegable list:** the projection sent to the phone carries opaque tokens and display strings only — asserted field by field that no `accessToken`, no refresh token, no sha and no account id appears (mutation proof: leaking the desktop's account id turns it red).
- **S9b per-lane usage pull (Q7)** — `lane-usage-pull.test.ts`: a preparation is built per **loaded** lane and `resolveOAuthCredentialReadOptions` receives that lane's `configDir` (`claude-fetcher.ts:236-248`); a lane with no credential loaded is skipped, not probed. **The three interactions, each with its own mutation proof:** a probe running in lane A **defers** rotation of A's account — it registers a synthetic gate id through `markClaudePtySpawned` (`live-pty-gate.ts:97-102`) and releases it on every exit path — and does not defer another lane's (mutation proofs: not registering the probe turns it red and a double rotation becomes observable; registering through the **persisting** arm turns a second test red, which asserts the synthetic id never reaches `persistence.addClaudeLivePtySessionId` (`:101`) and so is never seeded back at startup, §2k); a probe that starts while `isClaudeAuthSwitchInProgress(A)` is **skipped and not queued** (mutation proof: queueing it turns a named test red by posting usage for the pre-switch account); **and the two start-side preconditions of the close-wipe fence (§2f, §2k, rev 15): no probe is started for a lane whose wipe is pending or in progress, and none for a lane whose `laneState` is `absent`** (**mutation proof:** removing the wipe-pending precondition turns a named test red in which the tick immediately after a completed sweep starts a probe whose `claude` writes a rotated `.credentials.json` into the lane the host has already reported wiped — the kill-only fence passes that test while the lane re-loads); and the post-probe `syncLane` runs over a lane that was probed on this tick **even though it has no live PTYs at that moment** — the named fixture is an **idle** lane whose only PTY was the probe itself, and a rotation the probe's own CLI performed must move the watermark so the desktop's next push is **not** refused `push_stale` (mutation proofs: running the sync *before* the probe turns it red — the rev-5 watermark bug in a new place; and re-imposing trigger (2)'s live-PTY precondition on the post-probe run turns *the idle fixture* red while a busy-lane fixture stays green, which is the exact hole an ordering-only assertion would have passed over). **A push landing mid-probe:** a push applied while a probe is in flight in that lane marks the probe **stale** — its result is dropped, it posts no usage row, it emits no `cli-observed` receipt and it moves no watermark, and its `claude` is **killed** through `cleanupHiddenRateLimitPty(term, termDisposables, { kill: true })` (`hidden-pty-cleanup.ts:25-58`), asserted on the process and not only on the result (**mutation proofs:** attributing a stale probe's post turns a named test red, with the usage landing under the post-push account; and disposing only the `registerHiddenRateLimitPty` registration — rev 12's wording, a bare `Set.delete` at `hidden-pty-cleanup.ts:15-18` — turns a *second* test red, in which the surviving CLI rotates the pre-push single-use token and the lane's next launch fails `invalid_grant`). **The row join:** `ClaudeStatusLineRateLimits` carries `paneKey` and the ingest resolves pane → lane → account without consulting `configDir` (mutation proof: deleting the paneKey arm and falling back to the config-dir compare turns a **Windows-cased** fixture red, which is the §2m(4) drop); **and the probe's own env carries no inherited pane identity — the vector the paneKey key creates (§2k, round 3's third major):** with `ORCA_PANE_KEY` set in the host process env (Orca launched from an Orca terminal, the case `shared/pane-identity-env.ts:29-38` documents), the lane probe's `spawnEnv` at `rate-limits/claude-pty.ts:247-251` contains **none** of `PANE_IDENTITY_ENV_KEYS` (`shared/pane-identity-env.ts:15`) **[code]**, asserted on the `spawnEnv` object itself (**mutation proof:** dropping the scrub turns a named test red, in which the probe's statusline post carries the *other* principal's paneKey and the new join lands that lane's usage on the other principal's row — the exact misattribution Q7 asks the join to fix); a terminal row surfaces `laneAccountLabel` + `laneUsage` for the account its lane holds, re-points on a push with no row rebuild, and shows `laneUsage` **omitted** (not zero) to a grant of another principal (mutation proof: zeroing instead of omitting turns it red). **Windows arm:** `getManagedUsagePanelAuthPreparation` returns `null` on `win32` (`claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064`), so the Windows suite asserts the pull is **disabled** and the statusline path still attributes — the degradation, not a failure.
- **Windows-only controls** (`describe.runIf(process.platform === 'win32')`, skipped elsewhere and never faked green) — **DACL:** a freshly provisioned lane directory reads back protected, with exactly the three allowed SIDs at FullControl and no deny ACE; a simulated `ensureWindowsUserDataAclGrant` (`icacls <userData>\* /grant:r …(OI)(CI)(F)`) and a simulated `grantDirAcl` on the lane root leave it protected (mutation proof: creating the lane through the async best-effort path, `secure-file.ts:59-73`, turns both red); `restrictWindowsPathSync` returning false makes provisioning **fail closed** (mutation proof: ignoring the return value turns it red); **and the rule is scoped to a native lane — an S9e lane root matched by `parseWslUncPath` (`shared/wsl-paths.ts:6-17`) skips the DACL step entirely and fails closed on the in-distro `chmod 0700/0600` read-back instead**. **Which half of that runs in which slice — rev 16, round 4's fourth major, because gate (v) below makes these controls a merge gate and S9e is deferred:** the **skip** is asserted in **S9a** (feed the provisioner a `wsl.localhost` candidate and assert `restrictWindowsPathSync` is never called and provisioning does not fail on a DACL), while the **read-back** half **activates with S9e** and is skipped — not faked green — until that slice lands, because the in-distro `chmod`/read-back lives in `claude-accounts/principal-credential-lane-wsl.ts`, which §6 assigns to S9e. Gate (v) therefore binds S9a to the skip arm only (**mutation proof:** rev 13's unconditional rule turns the S9a skip fixture red, because `restrictWindowsPathSync` returns `false` on a path it cannot set a DACL on (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:60-63`) **[code]** and every S9e lane is then refused — the twin of the UNC-refusal conflict rev 13 fixed on the other rule and not this one). **Write:** a push publishes through `renameFileWithWindowsRetry` and succeeds against a held-open target inside the retry window, while a bare `renameSync` throws (proving the combined writer of §2m(2) is load-bearing); a target held open **past** the window is refused `accounts.lane.push_write_locked` with the lane file and its watermark byte-identical (**mutation proof:** letting the rethrow at `fs-utils.ts:220` escape untyped turns it red — the client gets a raw `EBUSY` with no sentence, the class §3's Rule-3 row forbids); the wipe removes **both** temp shapes — `${target}.${pid}.${uuid}.tmp` and `${target}.${pid}.${ms}.${hex}.tmp` — and the second temp left by the EPERM retry path. **Containment:** an 8.3 short-name form, a drive-letter case flip, a `\\?\` extended form and a planted **junction** into a lane are each refused, and the symlink check is asserted to run **before** the directory check (mutation proof: reverting any check to `realpathSync` + case-sensitive compare turns a named test red) — **including `resolveOwnedClaudeManagedAuthPath` (`managed-auth-path.ts:36-42`) **[code]**, which §2m(4) now decides adopts the same module rather than being named and left alone**; a **remote** UNC lane root is refused at provisioning, while a `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…` root is **not** refused by the Windows-side check — that half is **S9a**, and it is what gate (v) binds S9a to — and is instead **routed** to §2n's in-distro canonicalization, whose fixture **activates with S9e** and is skipped until then, since the canonicalizer is S9e code (**mutation proof:** an unscoped UNC refusal — rev 12's rule — turns the **S9a** recognise-and-do-not-refuse fixture red, which is how the two rules were found unable to ship together). **Env case:** a lane create supplying `claude_config_dir` / `Claude_Config_Dir` in `env`, in `envToDelete`, and in `launchConfig.agentEnv` is stripped or refused (mutation proof: exact-case comparison turns all three red); **and the collapse is asserted on the env the *provider* hands the process, not on `spawnOptions.env` — round 3's third blocker:** with the **host process env** carrying `Claude_Config_Dir` (row 19, `runtime-paths.ts:14-25`) **[code]** and the lane patch carrying `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, the object the child receives carries **exactly one** casing and its value is the lane's (**mutation proofs:** collapsing at `computeLaneLaunch` instead — rev 13's site — turns it red, because `...mergeGitConfigEnvProtocol(stripInheritedBuildModeEnv(process.env), args.env)` (`local-pty-provider.ts:675-676` → `git-credential-prompt-env.ts:11`) **[code]** reinstates the inherited spelling *after* the anchor and Win32 resolves the **first** case-insensitive key in block order (`pty/windows-path-segment-merge.ts:18`) **[code]**, so the pane silently runs on `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`; and omitting the collapse from the **local** provider while adding it to the daemon turns a second red, since only the daemon has the `collapseWindowsPathEnvKeys` precedent today, `daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:163-190` called at `:833`); **and the same fix on the one call site that never passes through `computeLaneLaunch`, in *both* halves — rev 15 asserted only the first, which is round 4's second major.** (a) A lane usage probe launched with a lowercase `anthropic_api_key` in the host process env must have it stripped by `applyClaudeEnvPatch` — the strip loop at `environment.ts:36`, inside the `stripAuthEnv` block `:35-39` — before `rate-limits/claude-pty.ts:247-251` spawns, asserted on the `spawnEnv` object itself (mutation proof: reverting that loop to exact-case turns it red, and the probe's usage is then attributable to the wrong account, §2m(5)). (b) **The lane-key collapse is asserted on the probe's `spawnEnv` too:** with the host process env carrying `Claude_Config_Dir` (row 19) and the lane's `authPreparation` supplying `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, the object handed to `pty.spawn` (`claude-pty.ts:303-311`) carries **exactly one** casing and its value is the lane's (**mutation proof:** omitting the collapse from this call site while keeping it in the provider and the daemon turns it red — `applyClaudeEnvPatch` writes by exact key at `environment.ts:44-46` **[code]** over a plain spread, so both keys survive and Win32 resolves the inherited `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`; the fixture then asserts the three downstream writes §2k names — the paneKey join, the synthetic gate id and the post-probe `syncLane` watermark — all land on the lane's row while the probe holds the shared credential). **WSL classifier:** a lane pane whose shell resolves to `wsl.exe` is refused in the lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm — asserted on the provider path *and* on the daemon twin, **and, rev 16, on both routes to that resolution: once from the host `terminalWindowsShell` setting (path A), once from a per-tab `pty:spawn` `shellOverride: 'wsl.exe'` (path B, `ipc/pty.ts:5923` → `:6074`/`:6167` → `local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:41`) **[code]**, which is row 36 and the edge every restored lane pane re-enters through, **and — rev 17, row 37 — once from `args.projectRuntime` of `kind: 'wsl'` (`ipc/pty.ts:6076`/`:6169` → `local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:52-57`) **[code]**, the arm that resolves to `wsl.exe` *regardless of* the per-tab override and therefore outranks the input row 36 enumerates** (**mutation proof:** asserting the refusal over `shellOverride` alone leaves the `projectRuntime` fixture green while the pane runs in WSL — the same shape as the path-A-only hole one clause up)** (**mutation proof:** asserting the refusal only over the setting leaves the path-B fixture green while the pane runs in WSL) — and **no** lane path reaches `addWslEnvKeys` (`local-pty-provider.ts:762-764`, `pty-subprocess.ts:762-764`) (**mutation proof:** siting the refusal at `computeLaneLaunch` instead turns it red, because the resolved shell is not in `PtySpawnOptions` and the fixture's pane is one the *anchor* classifies as host and the *provider* resolves to `wsl.exe` — the 22-line classifier gap of §2m(5), made into a test); after S9e the same pane carries a **Linux** lane path under that distro's lane root and nothing else (mutation proof: deleting the typed assertion turns it red). **Resolved shell is per-pane (row 36, rev 16):** a lane `pty:spawn` carrying `shellOverride: 'pwsh.exe'` and one carrying `'cmd.exe'` each spawn with the lane's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` intact and are **not** refused, and the managed statusline is the `cmd` variant in both (`statusline-script.ts:17-18`) **[code]**, so §2k's attribution is identical (**mutation proof:** treating the host `terminalWindowsShell` setting as the pane's shell — rev 15's reading — turns these red, because the setting names Git Bash or PowerShell while the pane resolved to neither). **And the assertion's scope, its own control:** a **lane-less** managed-WSL account pane still launches with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` = that account's `wslLinuxAuthPath` under `claude-accounts/` (`runtime-auth-service.ts:693`, `claude-accounts/service.ts:939`) and is **not** refused (mutation proof: applying the lane-root assertion to every WSL-branch spawn rather than to lane-pinned ones turns this red — it is how S9e would break the feature it is layered on).
- **S9d UI + handoff** — presence rows carry `credentialLane`/`laneState`; the owner label resolves through the participant join (§2h). **The designation surface (rev 19):** it lists exactly the principal's bound grants, in any runtime state, and a designated lane still at `laneState: 'absent'` renders *"no push received from \<label\>"* rather than an empty state (mutation proof: rendering a generic "not provisioned" string turns it red — the human must be able to see *which* grant they ticked is silent). `openInMyLane`: grant B invoking it against grant A's terminal creates under **B's** lane; a caller with no lane is refused; **a caller with no *attachment* on the source ptyId is refused, while a merely-registered connection is also refused** — which proves the check is anchored to `attachmentsOf` and not `registerConnection`; an over-long or control-character seed prompt is rejected. **And the join is asserted at the principal, not the grant:** a person's **phone**, attached to a pane their own **desktop** grant is also attached to, is **allowed**, while a grant of the *other* principal attached to the same pane is refused (**mutation proof:** comparing `.pairedDeviceId` to `ctx.pairedDeviceId` — rev 12's wording — turns the phone case red while leaving the cross-principal case green, which is exactly how a grant-keyed join passes an inattentive suite). **Mutation proof:** swapping the attachment join back to a participants lookup turns a named test red.
- **Cross-version** — `tests/e2e/cross-version-wire/cross-version-terminal-wire.unit.test.ts` stays green (Rule 1 additive fields); the harness covers the terminal stream only (`docs/reference/remote-wire-compatibility.md:101-104`) **[code]**, so the Rule 3 changes (#3, #4, #5) need their own old-client test.

**Live verification on the real box** (the only proof of the §4 pinned assumption, and the gate on which shape of R2 ships). (0) Provision a **fresh** lane and open its first terminal — it must reach a usable prompt with at most the expected per-lane trust prompt, and `echo $CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` must print the lane path. (0b) **The plain-shell case:** open a lane pane with *no* command, confirm `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is the lane, then type `claude` and confirm it starts in the lane account — this is the §2a blocker, and it is invisible to any agent-launch-only probe. (1) `/status` in a live session, note the *Login method* / account row. (2) Switch accounts on the desktop. (3) Re-run `/status` in the **same** session — the account row must change with no restart; if not, the §4 fallback applies and `laneState: 'restart-required'` ships. (3b) Switch **again**, to a third account, and confirm the second switch also applies (the watermark regression, §2c). (4) `claude auth status --json` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to the lane confirms file-level identity — Orca already shells exactly this (`claude-accounts/service.ts:232-237`; `runClaudeCommand` `:1055-1060` sets the config dir from its argument) **[code]**. (5) The other developer's open terminal, checked the same way, is unchanged. (6) Start an agent through `terminal.createAgentSession`, through `orchestration.workerStart` with `worktree: 'current'`, through `orchestration.workerStart` with `worktree: 'new-child'`, and — the round-8 blocker's live proof — through `session.tabs.createTerminal` from the phone **with the desktop window open and focused**, and confirm **all four** report the lane account. (7) `ls -l` the lane after disconnecting the grant — `.credentials.json` **and any `*.tmp` sibling** gone; then `kill -9` the runtime with a lane loaded, restart, and check again — also gone, and the stale re-push refused (§2f). (7b) **OpenClaude, the open question:** run `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<lane> openclaude` on the pinned build and check which store it reads (`~/.openclaude` vs the lane) — if it honors the variable, §2a's refusal relaxes to an allowlist line; until this passes, lane launches of it stay refused. (9) **Windows, DACL read-back:** `Get-Acl` the lane directory — `AreAccessRulesProtected` true, exactly the current user, `S-1-5-18` and `S-1-5-32-544`, all FullControl, no deny. (10) **Windows, credential store (§2m(3), the gate):** provision two lanes, log in to a different account in each, delete one lane's `.credentials.json`, and assert that lane's `claude auth status --json` reports **no** account rather than the other lane's — if the Windows CLI keeps any store outside `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, this is where it shows, and until it passes Windows lane provisioning stays **off**. (11) **Windows, rename under lock:** push to a lane while a live `claude` in it holds `.credentials.json` open, and confirm the push succeeds within the retry window rather than throwing. (12) **Windows, containment:** attempt `orca account add --config-dir` against the lane root through an 8.3 form, a drive-letter case flip, a `\\?\` form and a junction — all four refused. (12b) **Windows shells:** run step (0b) three times — under **PowerShell** and **Git Bash**, the two shells the owner named (§2m(8)), and under `cmd`, the resolver's middle fallback — **and drive at least one of the three through a per-tab `shellOverride` rather than the host setting (row 36, rev 16), since path B is how every restored lane pane resolves its shell (`ipc/pty.ts:5923` → `:6074`/`:6167`) **[code]** and the setting is not what decides there** — and note that (0b) is read **inside** the pane, after the shell's profile has run, so a `$PROFILE` or `.bashrc` that rewrites `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` turns it red rather than passing silently (§2m(8), §4). (12c) **Windows, Git Bash environment passthrough (§2m(8), the gate on that shell):** in a **Git Bash** lane pane, assert `echo "$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR"` prints the **Windows** lane path unconverted (not `/c/Users/…`) and that `claude auth status --json` from that pane reports the lane's account; then repeat with `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` set and with it unset, to establish that the argument converter is not what carries the value. If it fails, Git Bash lane panes are **refused** `terminal.lane_shell_unsupported` at the lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm until the lane preparation carries a Git-Bash arm — the same fail-closed shape as the WSL refusal, and the reason this is a live step rather than a note is that the failure is silent: the pane still renders `credentialLane: 'grant'` while running outside its lane. **Every one of (9)–(12c) is Windows-only and cannot execute on the Linux VPS CI** (§2m(7)): they need a Windows runner or the owner's own box, run by hand before S9b merges. (8) **macOS, on the pinned version:** delete the lane's scoped Keychain item, launch into that lane, and assert the CLI **refuses** rather than falling back to the unsuffixed service (§2i); until this passes, macOS lane provisioning stays off.

## 6. Implementation slices

Ceiling is 300 lines for `.ts` (`.oxlintrc.json:138-143`), **400 for `.tsx`** (`:144-149`, the glob at `:145`, the rule at `:147` — the one S9b's `mobile/` line turns on) and 800 for tests (`:156-161`) **[code]**; `mobile/.oxlintrc.json` inherits all three (`"extends": ["../.oxlintrc.json"]`) **[code]**. Seven files this design edits are already on the ratchet — `runtime-auth-service.ts` (1806, `config/max-lines-baseline.txt:29`), `claude-accounts/service.ts` (1275, `:31`), `ipc/pty.ts` (7887, `:99`), `src/main/persistence.ts` (`:120`), `runtime/orca-runtime.ts` (`:141`), `runtime/rpc/methods/terminal.ts` (`:149`) and `AccountsPane.tsx` (`:240`) **[code]**. The ratchet is a *file list*, not a per-file count (`config/max-lines-baseline.txt:1-4`) **[code]**, so growing them breaks no CI rule and no AGENTS.md rule — but the discipline applies to **all seven**: each edit is net-neutral or paired with an extraction, and nothing new lands in their bodies that could live in a new module. **One file this design edits is under a *hard* ceiling rather than the ratchet, and rev 21 states the difference because the mobile budget below turns on it (round 9's second major):** `mobile/app/h/[hostId]/accounts.tsx` is **not** on the baseline list, so it has no grandfathered budget at all — it is bound by the flat 400, and it cannot be given one, because a per-file `max-lines` bump in `mobile/.oxlintrc.json` is forbidden by name (`AGENTS.md:16`) and `config/max-lines-baseline.txt:1-4` restates the remedy as *"split the oversized file instead"* **[code]**. There the extraction is not a discipline but the only legal way to land the edit, and S9b budgets it.

- **S9c — lifecycle + per-lane gate**. New: `claude-accounts/principal-lane-lifecycle.ts` (~170, close/idle/startup/revoke wipes keyed by principal + the `laneWipePending` publish/clear pair of §2f — **published here and read by S9b's `lane-usage-pull.ts` start-side refusal, which is the other half of the fence (§2k)** + watermark ordering + **the in-flight-probe fence: abort every probe in the lane through S9b's controller, confirm the process is dead, and only then sweep — a wipe that reports done while a probe can still write `.credentials.json` back is the failure §2f names**). Edits: `live-pty-gate.ts` (key gate and live set by lane; 101 lines today) with its four spawn-side reads (`ipc/pty.ts:4527`, `:4619`, `:6154`, `:6203`), `claude-accounts/service.ts:572-582` (thread a lane through `syncRuntimeAuthWithLivePtyGate`, today the **only** caller of `beginClaudeAuthSwitch` at `:576`) **and the push handler as its second caller**, `runtime-auth-service.ts:258`/`:428`/`:460` (the `hasLiveClaudePtys()` call sites become the account-scoped liveness query), `src/main/persistence.ts:7228-7282` (+lane per session id, +watermark rows; the binding-row lane itself lands in S9a at `:6993-7027`), `index.ts:2241-2250` and `daemon-init.ts:1043-1061` (lane-aware seed/confirm; the startup wipe orders **after** the lane sync and **before** the seed), `runtime-rpc.ts:1408-1409` (wipe on last close via `socket.device.deviceId`) and the two revoke paths `:638-656`/`:658-668` — each resolving `principalOf(deviceId)` **before** its `removeDevice` (`:648`, `:660`) and wiping only when no bound row survives it, rather than hooking beside `forgetGrant` (`:653`, `:666`) where the row is already gone.

It filters on nothing and asserts nothing about device kind. Also — stated in rev 20 so §2l step 1 has an owner in some slice — the **delegable-account curation tick** (~20): per account, "let my phone switch to this one", the owner-curated allowlist §7 question 7 records, whose projection S9b sends and whose phone-side rendering S9b's `mobile/` line budgets. - **S9e — the WSL-visible lane (§2n)**, independently shippable and **not** to be landed with S9a. Presence reuses `credentialLane: 'grant'`; `'wsl'` narrows to "WSL pane with no lane". **Cost is dominated by tests, not lines:** every §5 live step must be re-run inside WSL on the Windows host, because §2m(5)'s two classifiers can disagree on one pane. **Deferred by the owner's answer (2026-08-22): native shells only (§2m(8)), so S9e is out of the S9 delivery order and out of every gate in this section** — not descoped, and not blocking anything. It stays designed and independently shippable; the revival trigger is a WSL-forced pane (§2n), not a preference.
