**Hard platform gates, restated as gates and not notes.** (i) **macOS** lane provisioning stays **off** until §5 step (8) passes on the pinned CLI (§2i) — unchanged from rev 10. (ii) **Windows** lane provisioning stays **off** until §5 step (10) establishes that the Windows CLI keeps no credential state outside `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (§2m(3)); this is the same failure class as the macOS one — every lane silently collapsing onto one credential — and it is graded **[inferred]**, so it gets the same treatment. (iii) **Windows** lane provisioning additionally fails closed on an unverified DACL (§2m(1)), per lane, at creation — **on a *native* lane root only**, the same carve-out §2m(1), §2m(4), §2n(i), §3's degradation table and §5 all carry and rev 16's audit of this section left out of these two sentences (round 5 minor): a `parseWslUncPath` root (`shared/wsl-paths.ts:6-17`) **[code]** skips the DACL step and fails closed on the in-distro `chmod 0700/0600` read-back instead, because `restrictWindowsPathSync` returns `false` on any throw (`shared/secure-path-windows-acl.ts:60-63`) **[code]** and the unscoped rule therefore refuses every S9e lane. Dormant, since S9e is deferred. (iv) The **per-lane usage pull** ships disabled on `win32` (§2k Fact 2). (v) The Windows-only §5 controls and live steps (9)–(12c) **cannot run on the Linux VPS CI** and require a Windows runner or the owner's box; a slice does not merge with them unrun, and they must not be reported green by being skipped (§2m(7)). **Scoped to the slice that implements them — rev 16, because as written this gate contradicted the S9e deferral two bullets above it.** Two of the Windows-only controls have halves that only **S9e** can satisfy: the DACL arm's in-distro `chmod 0700/0600` **read-back**, and the containment arm's **routing** of a `wsl.localhost` root into §2n's in-distro canonicalizer — both live in `claude-accounts/principal-credential-lane-wsl.ts`, an S9e module. **S9a's gate covers the halves S9a implements** — the DACL **skip** for a `parseWslUncPath` candidate, and the Windows-side check **recognising** that root rather than refusing it — and the two S9e halves **activate with S9e**, skipped until then and never reported green. Nothing else in (9)–(12c) is S9e-dependent.

**Parallelism:** S9a's env/arg hardening and capture containment land first and alone; S9a's funnel plus the `isClaudeLaunch` decoupling is a single mechanical change best landed whole (the compiler enumerates the call sites); the S9b transport skeleton runs in parallel; S9b's refusals depend on S9a's provisioning seam; S9c depends on S9a; S9d depends on S9b; **S9b's phone-switch RPC depends on S9a's principal registry and on nothing else**, and **S9b's per-lane pull depends on S9c's per-lane gate**, so if the slices are reordered the pull moves with the gate, not with the push. **S9e depends on S9a and on nothing later**, which is what lets it be *deferred* cleanly rather than descoped: nothing in S9b–S9d waits on it, and reviving it later costs no re-sequencing. **Hard gates:** no slice merges without its mutation proofs; the §5 live verification — steps (0b) and (3b) especially — runs before S9b merges and **selects** R2's shape rather than blocking it (§4).

## 7. Owner decisions, and what is still open

**Q1–Q10 are RESOLVED (owner, 2026-08-22). S9 is approved in full: S9a–S9d all build.** Each answer is recorded with the consequence it had on this document, so a later reader can tell a decision from a default.

| # | Question | Owner's answer | Consequence in rev 11 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cross-grant delegation for one human | **YES** — *"if we use mobile it should be an extension of our desktops targeting the same shared dev box"* | The lane key becomes a host-minted **`principalId`** and a lane belongs to a **person**; grants are bound to a principal only by the host-side consent action, never by `--pair-name` (§2a). Every lane-keyed structure re-keys, the wipe predicate is rebuilt because B2's is grant-scoped, and the adopt/materialize gate now lets a person's phone wake their desktop's pane. The hard half — **who pushes from the phone** — is answered in §2l: the phone selects, the desktop pushes, the host refuses `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` when the desktop is offline. Rev 10's default ("no — one grant, one lane") is **overridden**, and it is the only rev-10 decision this revision reverses |
| 2 | Rotated-token return path | **Pull it back on every receipt** | §2c/§2e unchanged in mechanism, confirmed in intent: the desktop pulls the rotated blob over the grant-scoped channel on every `host` and `cli-observed` receipt, so the desktop store never goes stale and the post-wipe re-push cannot restore a revoked blob. `accounts.lane.pullRotated` (§3 row 2) is now a required part of S9b, not an option |
| 3 | Identity label the other developer sees | **Grant/owner label + a friendly per-account name chosen by the owner** | The presence row and the projection carry the **principal** label (host-observed, unspoofable) plus an owner-authored per-account name. The **email is not shown by default** — it stays available only where the owner explicitly opts in, which is the right default given §2b's finding that the email is client-asserted. `laneAccountLabel` (§2k) is exactly this field |
| 4 | Handoff scope-out | **Self-switch continuity yes; cross-person handoff stays out** — *"if we're switching accounts for ourselves we want to continue the conversation, just with a different claude account"* | The half the owner wants is **native and needs no feature**: same lane, same `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, same transcripts, the running session re-resolves (§2g, new opening). §2g's cross-person scope-out **stands unchanged**, enforced by the containment refusal |
| 5 | Shared-lane switching | **NO — do not lock it** | The shared lane stays open to lane-less grants; the residual is **decided and named in full** in §2d, with the procedural mitigation (bind and provision both developers before either relies on the box) stated rather than implied |
| 6 | Lane-strict mode | **OFF** | §4's option is not built. R1 remains an **authority** guarantee, not a confidentiality one, and every seam continues to say so |
| 7 | Lane usage bars | **Per-person, per-account, and per the account attached to the terminal/agent** | The per-lane usage **pull moves into S9b** and is designed in §2k: per-lane preparation, the three interactions with refresh deferral / the lane gate / the watermark each with a mutation proof, and a `paneKey → lane → account → usage` row join that replaces the config-dir string compare. Two Rule-1 fields, `laneAccountLabel` and `laneUsage`. **Gated on Windows** by `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` |
| 8 | Cross-grant orchestration cut | **OK, confirmed** | §2a's ownership predicate ships as written, now comparing **principals** — which softens the cut exactly where it was most annoying: your own phone can drive your own coordinator pane |
| 9 | Lane session forking, mirror list | **OK, confirmed** | §2a's loss list and mirror allowlist ship item by item, asserted individually in §5. Forking is intended, not a regression to work around |
| 10 | OpenClaude in a lane | **Block it** | §2a's fail-closed refusal ships: `terminal.lane_agent_unsupported` for a lane launch, and a hand-typed session downgrades the row to `credentialLane: 'shared-runtime'` with its usage dropped. The §5 step (7b) probe can still retire the question, but the shipped default is the block |

**Host pivot, recorded as a decision rather than a question.** The shared dev machine will run **Windows Server**; both developers use Windows 11 desktops; the owner's personal VPS stays Linux and single-owner and is out of scope; the owner installs the Windows build on the shared machine personally. Consequences are §2m (a first-class section replacing rev 10's paragraph), §2n (S9e), the Windows gates in §6, and the honest statement in §2m(7) that the live probes cannot run on the Linux VPS CI. **Second half of the pivot, answered 2026-08-22 and recorded here as a decision rather than a question: the shells are native — Git Bash (the owner) and PowerShell (the other developer), deliberately not WSL.** Consequences are §2m(8) (the two shell facts, one graded [inferred] and gated by §5 step (12c), one a §4 residual), row 18 (the setting is host-wide, so both shells are in scope for every lane), the deferral of S9e out of the delivery order and out of every gate (§2n, §6), and §7 question 1, now resolved.

### New questions raised by the host pivot — two now answered (1 and 8), the rest open

1. **RESOLVED (owner, 2026-08-22): native Windows shells, not WSL — and the consequence is a *deferral*, not a descope.** The question was which shells the two developers' agents run in on the shared box. The answer: **native terminals only — the owner uses Git Bash, the other developer uses PowerShell, deliberately not WSL.** So S9a–S9d isolate everything that matters for daily work, and **S9e (§2n) is deferred**: a designed, independently shippable slice, **out of the S9 delivery order and out of every gate in §6**, with nothing in S9b–S9d waiting on it. No "BLOCKING" framing survives anywhere in this document. **Rev 16 closes the one place that claim was not yet true:** §6 gate (v) made the Windows-only §5 controls an S9a merge gate, and two of those controls had halves only S9e implements — the DACL arm's in-distro `chmod` read-back and the containment arm's routing into §2n's canonicalizer. Each is now split: the half S9a implements (the DACL **skip** for a `parseWslUncPath` candidate; the Windows-side check **recognising** that root rather than refusing it) gates S9a, and the S9e half **activates with S9e**, skipped and never faked green (§5, §6 gate (v)). **Two obligations came *back* from the answer rather than going away, and both are §2m(8):** Git Bash's MSYS conversion — it rewrites path-looking *arguments* and passes the environment block through, which is what lets a Windows lane path reach a native `claude.exe` from a Git Bash pane, graded **[inferred]**, gated by §5 step **(12c)** and carrying a named refusal (`terminal.lane_shell_unsupported`) if the probe fails — and PowerShell's **`$PROFILE`**, which runs at pane start, i.e. after every guard in this design, and is therefore a §4 residual of the shell-rc class, named and not closed. **And the answer is not an enforcement:** a project runtime of kind `wsl` still forces a WSL shell (`shared/local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:52-57`) **[code]**, where a lane pane is *refused* (`terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported`) rather than silently un-isolated — which is exactly what makes deferring S9e safe rather than optimistic.
2. **The Windows credential-store probe is a gate on the whole thing (§2m(3), §5 step (10)).** Orca's own code treats `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/.credentials.json` as the entire store on Windows, and nothing in the tree observes whether the Windows CLI *also* keeps a DPAPI or Credential Manager entry. Graded **[inferred]**. Until the probe runs on the owner's box, Windows lane provisioning is off — which means **S9 cannot be used on the target host at all**. Who runs it, and when? It is a twenty-minute manual check and it gates everything.
3. **Windows administrators are inside every lane's ACL by construction (§2m(6)).** The hardening script grants FullControl to the user, SYSTEM and `S-1-5-32-544` (Administrators). If both developers are local administrators on the shared box — the default for a machine they both install software on — then "co-tenant can read a resident lane file" holds for either of them regardless of which Windows account `orca serve` runs under. Accept it as the §4-class residual it is, or make one developer a non-administrator on that box (which changes nothing about R1 and everything about the residual)?
4. **Two desktops in one principal, and who holds the lease.** §2e's delegation lease is **host-published** and, since rev 14, **written only by the provisioning consent action**: it names one pushing desktop per principal, refuses the others `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated`, and — the part that actually closes the second rotator — is the signal every other bound desktop reads to suppress its own token refresh. Rev 13's alternative writer ("pinned from the first accepted push") is deleted, because an authority value written by a client action lets any grant of the principal seize the lease by racing (§2e). Two consequences to confirm. **(a)** A lane cannot be provisioned for a principal **none of whose bound grants has been designated as its pusher** — refused `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated` (§2a, §2e). **Rev 19 changes what that designation *is*, and it changes what you are being asked to accept, so it is restated here rather than left in §2a.** Revs 15–18 tried three times to have the *host* work out which grant is a pushing desktop — from `scope`, then from a capability the host advertises, then from one the client declares plus a memory of it — and the orchestrator has ruled that out: **the designation is a plain tick, by you, over the grants already bound to that principal, available at any time on the same local UI (or the same local `orca` command) that binds them, and persisted like a binding.** For you and one desktop that is **one tick**. The host makes **no** judgement about what kind of device a grant is; ticking your phone, or an `orca` CLI grant, is allowed and **inert** — no push arrives, the lane sits at `laneState: 'absent'`, the UI says *"no push received from \<label\>"*, and you re-tick. What you are accepting is therefore a recovery story rather than a prevention story, on a surface only someone at the machine can reach. Correct, or should provisioning be allowed to sit in a designation-less, permanently-`absent` state until you get round to it? **(b)** Is a manual "move the lease to this desktop" control in the UI enough, or do you want the lease to follow the most recently active desktop automatically? Automatic is friendlier and is exactly the R-DR2 shape that revokes tokens out from under live sessions, so the design chose manual; confirm.
5. **Revoking one device of a person does not wipe the lane (§2a).** Only the principal's last grant does. That is correct under Q1 and it is also surprising — "I revoked the lost phone, is my account still on that box?" The answer is yes, until the desktop disconnects. Should the revoke dialog offer a **"and wipe the lane now"** checkbox, which is a one-line addition to S9c's revoke path?
6. **Per-lane usage pull cost on the shared box.** §2k's pull is `N` hidden `claude` processes per tick, one per loaded lane, each holding that lane's token and deferring its rotation for the probe's lifetime. On this box `N ≤ 3` and the tick interval is unchanged, but every probe is a real process on a machine two people are working on. Acceptable as designed, or should the pull run at a **slower cadence than the shared-lane fetch** — say, only when a lane's row is actually on screen?
7. **The delegable-account list is an owner-curated allowlist (§2l).** The phone can request a switch only to accounts the owner has ticked. Is per-account ticking the interaction you want, or should every account on the desktop be delegable by default with an opt-*out*? Default-in is friendlier; default-out is the direction every other decision in this document went.

8. **RESOLVED (orchestrator, 2026-08-22): the federated link gets a principal binding — option (b). (§2a, raised in rev 13, answered in rev 15.)** The federated create (`orchestration-federation.ts:90`) has no parent pane (`lineage: { noParent: true }`, `:106`) and no `pairedDeviceId` — its only caller identity is a home-peer fingerprint (`:63`) **[code]** — so neither of §2a's derivations reaches it, and rev 12's "binds to the link's grant" named a key `credentialLane` has no case for. Rev 13–14 left it **failing closed**, which is correct and is also a functional cut on the primary federated-dispatch path: no link→principal binding exists in this tree, so with no registry write *every* federated worktree create is refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound`. Three options were put: (a) leave it closed and treat federated worktree creation as out of scope for S9 (rev 13–14's text); (b) add link→principal binding to `principal-registry.ts` as one more consent write, ~20 lines and one more thing the local human must tick per link; (c) let a federated create pass explicit `{ kind: 'shared' }`, which is the option this document refuses everywhere else and should be refused here too. **RESOLVED: (b), on the orchestrator's decision of 2026-08-22.** It ships as the **fifth** host-side consent write in the principal registry, beside bind / unbind / re-bind / designate-the-pusher, keyed by the link's **home-peer fingerprint** — the only stable link identity in this tree (`runtime/orchestration/types.ts:205`, column `runtime/orchestration/db.ts:504`, authorized against at `orchestration-federation-relay.ts:227` and `orchestration-federation-control.ts:175`) **[code]**. A federated create then binds `{ kind: 'principal', principalId }` from the link's binding **or fails closed** `terminal.lane_link_unbound` — never a grant, never an implicit `shared`. **The one extra human tick, stated because it is the whole of the cost:** each federated link that should be able to create worktrees is ticked once, on the same consent surface and with the same audit row as a device bind; an unticked link keeps rev 13's behaviour, which is the refusal. Option (c) stays refused for the reason it is refused everywhere else. §2a states it at the site; §6 carries it in `principal-registry.ts`; §5 asserts both arms. **Rev 16 corrects the *key*, and the correction is load-bearing rather than cosmetic.** Rev 15 called the home-peer fingerprint a link identity on the strength of where it is stored and compared. Re-opened at the function that produces it, `authenticatedCallerFingerprint` is `sha256(request.authToken || request.deviceToken || 'authenticated_transport')` (`rpc/orchestration-mutation-executor.ts:128-134`, spent at `:32`, threaded at `dispatcher.ts:100`) **[code]** — so it is the shared per-runtime `authToken` (`runtime-rpc.ts:507`, enforced `:1664-1669`) for **every** local caller including both developers on the one Windows account, and `sha256(deviceToken)` — per grant, rotated on re-pair (`device-registry.ts:97`) — for a paired peer. Binding a principal to it as written would have bound the *other developer's* local calls into that principal's lane. **The answer stays (b); the binding gains three clauses (§2a):** the two host-wide values (`sha256(authToken)`, `sha256('authenticated_transport')`) are refused by name; a bindable fingerprint must resolve to **exactly one** paired grant's `token`, and the row records `boundDeviceId` so the audit names the grant — **and, rev 17, records *only* that: the link row carries no `principalId` of its own, because the grant clause (b) resolves already has one and the create reads `principalOf(boundDeviceId)`, which removes a duplicate that could have disagreed with it and leaves one revocation point** — and a re-pair stops matching, so the link returns to `terminal.lane_link_unbound` until re-ticked — one more tick, and a refusal rather than a wrong-lane create in between. **Nothing else about the answer moves,** and the extra cost is bounded: federated creates from an anonymous local socket are now refused rather than bindable, which is the same direction every other decision here went.

9. **The lease now reaches your own desktop's `~/.claude` — rule (iv), new in rev 14 (§2e).** Rev 13's lease suppressed Orca's rotator and Orca's managed launches on every bound desktop, and left a third copy of the credential untouched: the **runtime credential file on the delegating desktop itself**, written by an ordinary `accounts.selectClaude` there (`runtime-auth-service.ts:477-478`) **[code]**. A plain `claude` in any non-Orca terminal on that Windows 11 desktop reads that file and rotates the lane's single-use refresh token, and neither the host's residency index nor that machine's live-PTY gate can see it — R-DR2 on the machine §2e calls safe. Rule (iv) therefore **clears the delegated account out of the delegating desktop's own runtime file** and refuses a local `selectClaude` of it while the lease stands. **The cost is on your daily machine, which is why it is a question and not only a decision:** while account X is delegated to the shared host, a bare `claude` in your own Windows terminal no longer runs as X — you would switch it back with `accounts.lane.clear`, or use a different account locally. Accept that, or would you rather rule (iv) be a **loud warning plus detection** (the `foreign-rotation` receipt path) instead of a clear-and-refuse, accepting the double-rotation risk on your own box?

11. **Does the owner accept that the host holds their *ticked* account list? (§2b, §3 row 12, new in rev 22.)** §2l's phone flow cannot exist without it — the phone must be shown something to tap, and §2b forbids the list riding the push — so `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` ships the list to the host, where it persists in `claudeLaneDelegationRows`. That makes §2b's old blanket sentence ("the host never receives the owner's account **list**") false, and it is corrected there rather than left standing. The exposure is bounded — only ticked accounts, only an opaque token plus an owner-authored `displayName` and an **opted-in** `email`, no credential and no store id — and §2d keeps the list readable only by that principal's own grants. But §4 grades a paired peer shell-equivalent on this box, so a co-tenant who is already shell-equivalent learns the owner's ticked account names and any opted-in email. Q3 answered the peer-visible *label*; it did not answer this. **Options:** (a) accept as shipped; (b) drop `email` from the write entirely and let the phone disambiguate on `displayName` alone; (c) hold the delegable list — and with it §2l's phone switch — until the owner rules. Shipped today as (a), flagged here for ratification.

### Still unresolved, and not by choice

- **Whether the Windows CLI honours `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` for *credentials* as completely as it does for config** is [inferred] and cannot be settled from this tree — question 2 above, §5 step (10). It is the single largest unresolved item, and it gates the target host.
- **Why `getManagedUsagePanelAuthPreparation` returns `null` on `win32`** (`rate-limits/claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064`) is unexplained in the code and unrecoverable from it. Until someone establishes the reason, the per-lane pull cannot be enabled on Windows on evidence — only on a guess, which this design declines to make (§2k Fact 2).
- **Whether OpenClaude honours `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`** remains the rev-10 [inferred], now moot for shipping (Q10 = block) but still worth the §5 step (7b) probe, because a positive result collapses a refusal into one allowlist line.
- **The `\\wsl.localhost` write path's failure modes under load** (§2n) are asserted from the 9p redirector's general behaviour, not observed here; S9e's first live step must measure a push through it rather than assume the Windows retry budget is sufficient.

## 8. Open review items (to resolve during implementation review)

Round 10 returned **zero blockers** and two majors. Both are recorded here verbatim rather than applied, and each is dispositioned to the slice review that must close it. **Items 1 and 2 are now closed in rev 22, during S9b review round 2; the dispositions below say how, and the item text above each is kept verbatim so the correction can be read against what was found.** **Rev 24 adds items 3–5, which are residuals the shipped S9b created or left standing (§9); they are open, not dispositioned away.**

1. **Round-9 major 1 was not applied at §2a; §2l cites §2a as already carrying a scope §2a does not write.**
   *Where:* `/home/ubuntu/orca-resilience-docs/docs/reference/agent-identity-s9-design.md:122` (§2a, "One residual the recovery does not cover") against `:313` (§2l) and `:403` (§3 degradation row).
   *Detail:* Rev 21's own preamble says round 9's first finding was "Corrected consistently at §2l, §5 and §3's degradation row" — §2a is not in that list, and it is the one site that still carries the pre-rev-21 scope. §2a:121 reads: "a designated grant that is *connected* but cannot act still satisfies §2l's liveness precondition, so a phone's `requestSwitch` is emitted rather than refused up front and runs to `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out`." Under rev 21 that general clause is false for the very grant class rev 21 added: a designated `orca` CLI grant is *connected* (its one-shot socket authenticates) and does NOT satisfy the precondition, because the precondition's second conjunct is a live lane-status subscription and the CLI has no subscribe entry point at all — verified in this tree: `src/cli/runtime/websocket-transport.ts` is 51 lines exporting only `sendWebSocketRequest` (`:11` → `:19`) and `sendWebSocketRequestWithStatusPreflight` (`:28` → `:38`), `grep -rn subscribe src/cli` returns zero hits, and `subscribeRemoteRuntimeRequest` (`src/shared/remote-runtime-client.ts:494`) has exactly one production caller, `src/main/ipc/runtime-environment-transport-routing.ts:246`. Worse, §2l:312 asserts the opposite of what §2a says: "The `switch_timed_out` residual is the connected-**and-subscribed** case and only that, which is the scope §2a:121 already writes with the words *'a designated grant that is **connected**'*." The words §2a writes are "connected", full stop — the citation claims support §2a does not give, and the sentence rev 21 points at is exactly the sentence that needed correcting. (The rev-17 narrative in the preamble at `:7` carries the same stale form: "a *connected* one that cannot act still expires `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out`".) Not a blocker because §2a states no predicate of its own and explicitly defers to §2l, and §2l/§3 row 9/§5/§6 all state the shipped rule consistently — but it is a wrong claim at a normative site and an unsupported self-citation, and it re-creates the exact conflation round 9 found. Fix: restate §2a:121 as "connected **and holding a lane-status subscription**", keep the phone as its example, and drop or reword §2l's "which is the scope §2a:121 already writes".
   *Disposition:* **RESOLVED in rev 22 (S9b review round 2).** §2a:121 now reads "connected **and holding a lane-status subscription**", names the `orca` CLI grant as the class the old clause was false for, and keeps the phone as its example; §2l's self-citation is re-worded to cite the corrected §2a rather than claim support the old one did not give; the rev-17 narrative in the preamble carries the same two conjuncts. The tree was already right — `lane-delegated-switch.ts` keys `assertDelegatedDesktopSubscribed` on `hasSubscriptionForGrant` — so this is a doc-only correction with no code change.

2. **§2l step 1's delegable-account list has no writer: no §3 wire row, no §2b envelope member, and its only budgeted owner lands in a later slice than the RPC that validates against it.**
   *Where:* `/home/ubuntu/orca-resilience-docs/docs/reference/agent-identity-s9-design.md:307` (§2l step 1) against `:172` (§2b envelope), `:390` (§3 row 9), `:478` (§6 S9b) and `:480` (§6 S9d).
   *Detail:* §2l step 1 requires that "the desktop publishes into its principal's lane state a **delegable account list**: per account the owner ticks, an opaque `delegatedAccountId` … plus the display fields §2b already permits", and step 2 has the host "validate the token against that principal's delegable list". Every other artefact in the document covers only the READ side of that list: §3 row 9 puts "the delegable-account list on the lane status stream" (host→client) and defines no method for the desktop→host write; §3's table is asserted to be the complete wire-change list. §2b closes the push envelope at exactly three members and refuses "an extra member" with `accounts.lane.push_malformed`, so the list cannot ride the push, and §2b states normatively that "The host never receives the owner's account **list**, ids of unselected accounts…". §6's S9b budgets `rpc/methods/lane-delegated-switch.ts` (~120) for "`accounts.lane.requestSwitch`, the delegable-account **projection**, the lease-holder liveness precondition … and the request timeout" — projection only — and §6's S9d budgets the desktop-side "delegable-account curation tick (~20)" in `AccountsPane.tsx`, with §6's own parallelism note stating "S9d depends on S9b". So on the shipped order S9b ships `requestSwitch` plus §5's fixtures for "a valid `delegatedAccountId`" and "an unknown or another principal's `delegatedAccountId` is refused" while no code path anywhere can make a `delegatedAccountId` exist: the host mints the tokens ("a host-side random token, minted with the lane") but is never told which accounts to mint them for. This is the same shape as round 8's major (a §2l obligation with no budget in any slice); rev 20 half-noticed it — §6:480 says the tick is "stated in rev 20 so §2l step 1 has an owner in some slice" — but assigned only the UI, not the wire method, the host-side store, its schema/bounds validation (the display fields are owner-authored strings crossing the wire, exactly what §2b bounds elsewhere), or a §5 write-side control. Fix: add a §3 row for the desktop→host delegable-list write with its own bounds validation, budget it in the slice that ships `requestSwitch`, and move the S9d tick's dependency accordingly (or state explicitly that `requestSwitch` refuses everything until S9d lands).
   *Disposition:* **RESOLVED in rev 22 (S9b review round 2), on the "add the rows" branch rather than the "hold the widening" one.** §3 gains **row 12** for `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` with its authorization, its bounds and its refusal codes; §6's S9b budgets `runtime/lane-delegation-directory.ts` for the host-side store the row needs, and states that S9d's curation tick depends on S9b (until the owner ticks, the list is empty and `requestSwitch` refuses every token by name). §2b's normative sentence is corrected: the host **does** now receive the owner's *ticked* list, bounded to an opaque token plus a `displayName` and an opted-in `email`. Because that is a privacy widening no owner decision covers — Q3 answered the peer-visible *label*, not the list — it ships flagged rather than silently: **§7 question 11** puts it to the owner with three options, including dropping `email` or holding §2l's phone switch. The code half (`setDelegableAccounts`, `parseDelegableAccountInputs`) already shipped in S9b sub-slice 3b.

3. **`lane-write-lost` is reported for a PARTIAL publish on win32 even when the lane holds a working credential (§9 item 19).**
   *Where:* `claude-accounts/lane-credential-writer.ts` (the published-path DACL verification arm).
   *Detail:* the code is fail-closed by choice — the host cannot vouch for that file's ACL after the rename — but the refusal's name asserts the lane received nothing, which on win32 is not always true. The docstring says so; the wire name does not.
   *Disposition:* **OPEN, owed to S9c.** Either the code is split into a second name for the ACL-unverified case, or §2i states that one name covers both and the client sentence carries the difference.

4. **L1's residency predicates have no production caller (S9b minor 5), and the consequence is written at both sites rather than fixed.**
   *Where:* `claude-accounts/managed-account-lane-residency.ts` / `runtime/rpc/methods/accounts.ts` (commit `f29d6e56db`).
   *Detail:* `assertNotLaneResident` is required from `accounts.selectClaude` **and** `accounts.removeClaude` for every caller class including the anonymous local one, and `assertAccountNotResidentElsewhere` from `accounts.lane.push`. All three call sites need the target account's residency keys derived from managed storage, which is 3b's push/accounts work. The docstrings now say L1 is unenforced, that the rotation gate rests on it, and that `findLaneResidency` never reports the shared lane — so a live shared-lane `claude` would not defer a lane's rotation. Unreachable today because no push RPC exists, so no lane can be loaded.
   *Disposition:* **OPEN, owed to 3b, which must land BOTH `accounts.ts` call sites and not only the push one.**

5. **The host now holds the owner's *ticked* account list, and it shipped ahead of the owner's answer (§9 item 24, §7 question 11).**
   *Where:* `runtime/lane-delegation-directory.ts` (`claudeLaneDelegationRows`).
   *Detail:* opaque token + owner-authored `displayName` + opted-in email; no credential and no store id. §2b's "the host never receives the owner's account list" was normative and is now corrected, but the widening is live before the decision that authorizes it.
   *Disposition:* **OPEN, owner decision.** If the answer is no, the row set has to shrink to tokens the desktop resolves, and §2l's phone rendering changes with it.

## 9. What shipped — S9a and S9b implementation deviations (rev 24)

**This section is the reconciliation.** Every item is a place where the *implementation* departed from §2–§6 as written, recorded as a decision with the reason it was taken. Nothing here is a proposal: it is all in the tree at the `feat/agent-identity-s9` tip. Where the code is right and this document was wrong, the sentence above has been corrected in place and the item says so.

### (a) S9a — env/arg hardening, capture containment, lanes, the funnel

1. **The pane lane is a discriminated union with `kind: 'principal' | 'shared'`, not a nullable `principalId`.** Reason: every consumer had to decide what "no lane" meant, and a union makes the shared lane a value the compiler forces each site to handle instead of a `null` that silently defaults.
2. **The `inherit` option carries the *caller*, not just the parent pane.** Reason: §2a's ownership predicate needs the principal doing the inheriting to refuse a split of someone else's pane; a parent-only option cannot express the refusal.
3. **Grant→principal bindings persist in the principal registry's own store (`runtime/principal-registry-store.ts:1-113`), and `DeviceEntry` is untouched.** Reason: §3 row 8 put `principalId` on `DeviceEntry`; a separate store keeps the device registry's corrupt-load path (`loadSucceeded`) and its orphan reconciliation independent of the binding table, which §2a requires to fail closed separately. **§3 row 8 is therefore wrong as written and is superseded by this item.**
4. **A WSL selection target on a lane-pinned launch is REFUSED, `terminal.lane_wsl_unsupported`.** Reason: §2a fell through to today's WSL branches; a fall-through would run a lane-pinned pane on an account-scoped, not lane-scoped, credential, which is the exact confusion the lane exists to prevent. S9e (§2n) is what lifts it.
5. **A lane-less caller splitting an *unattributed* pane resolves to the shared lane rather than being refused; a lane holder splitting one is refused.** Reason: refusing both would break every pre-existing pane on upgrade, and the asymmetry is safe — the shared lane is what an unattributed pane already ran in.
6. **A renderer-notified split fails closed, `terminal.lane_renderer_split_unsupported`.** Reason: the renderer path has no authenticated caller to derive a lane from, and §2a forbids taking lane identity from renderer-supplied input.
7. **The coordinator loop passes an explicit shared lane rather than omitting the argument.** Reason: `terminal.lane_unspecified` is fail-closed, so an omitted lane throws; the coordinator is host-internal and genuinely shared, and saying so is cheaper than a second nullable path.
8. **Anonymous and renderer-minted panes are bound to the shared lane at `registerPty`, not left unbound.** Reason: an unbound pane is indistinguishable from a lane pane whose registry row was lost, and the fail-closed lookup would then refuse legitimate local panes.
9. **The lane row lives in a `paneKey`-keyed map *beside* the binding row rather than inside it.** Reason: the binding row is persisted and the lane row is not always resolvable at persistence time; keeping them apart lets the lane be re-derived on load without a schema migration.
10. **`laneScopedAgentLaunchInputs` (`runtime/lane-launch-computation.ts:134-160`) is applied at six builders, not the seven `settings.agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv` consumption sites §2 row 13/14 enumerates** (`runtime/orca-runtime.ts:22321`, `:22399`, `:26139`, `:26290`, `:26463`, `:27647`). Reason: two of the seven old sites had collapsed into one builder by the time the change landed; the *set of launches* covered is unchanged.
11. **`runtime/lane-permission-narrowing.ts` (61) exists as its own module and preserves the host-wide narrowing.** Reason: §2a scoped permission narrowing to lanes; removing the host-wide arm would have silently widened every non-lane launch, so both arms ship and the module says which is which.
12. **Six refusal codes beyond §3's table**: `terminal.lane_remote_pane`, `terminal.lane_not_loaded` (two sentences, because the remedy differs by whether a lane exists at all), `accounts.lane.lane_path_not_contained`, `accounts.lane.lane_root_not_local`, `accounts.lane.provisioning_platform_gated` and `accounts.lane.grant_not_per_person`. Reason: each names a refusal §2 describes in prose but never gave a code, and an untyped throw reaches the person as nothing at all. §3's degradation row now carries the complete enumeration.
13. **`claude-accounts/managed-auth-path.ts` adopted the shared containment helper** (`shared/lane-path-containment.ts`, used at `managed-auth-path.ts:30-42`). Reason: §2m(4) required case-folded `realpathSync.native` containment for lanes; running two different containment rules in one tree is how the 8.3-short-name hole comes back.
14. **`agentArgs` is sanitized at `createAgentSession` *and* `resumeAgentSession`, not only at the anchor.** Reason: §2 row 8 put the allowlist at the anchor, but the two agent-session RPCs build their args before the anchor can see them (`lane-launch-computation.ts:148-160` says so in code).
15. **The spawn anchor is `spawnWithLane` in its own module (`ipc/lane-pinned-spawn.ts:21-27`), and `attachStablePaneOwner`'s reattach at `ipc/pty.ts:844` is deliberately NOT routed through it.** Reason: the reattach passes `attachOnly: true` with no command and then proves it reattached the same incarnation, so it creates nothing a lane computation could govern — and computing a lane over its bare `{ cols, rows, cwd }` would refuse a legitimate reattach whose lane happens to be unloaded. §2a's "all three `provider.spawn` calls" is corrected in place.

### (b) S9b — push, scoping, account-keyed refresh, attribution

16. **The reauth hold is a PERSISTED field on the watermark row (`ClaudeLaneCredentialWatermark.reauthRequired?: boolean`, additive, normalized from a literal `true` only), not the in-memory Set §2c/§2e imply.** Reason: the doc's watermark shape is secretless-by-design and so is this field, and the blocker only closes if the hold outlives both the next sync *and* a restart — an in-memory hold evaporates and writer 1 walks the watermark back onto the spent sha on the first post-restart launch. Deliberate side effect: it also closes round 1's concern-1 restart residual.
17. **Writer 1 no-ops while the lane is held for reauth.** §2c names writer 1 as recording what `syncLane` observed on all four triggers with no exception. Reason: without the exception the hold is undone by the very next sync — the reproduced blocker; only writer 2 (a push) or writer 3 (a rotation the lane received) lifts it.
18. **The sync driver also skips the rotation when the lane is held AND its file has not moved since the last observation.** §2c rotates on triggers 1–3 unconditionally. Reason: that file holds exactly the copy the last rotation spent, so rotating buys a network round trip and a refusal per launch with no path out. A moved file still rotates, so a real CLI rotation is not blocked.
19. **`lane-write-lost` keeps its name and its fail-closed direction for a PARTIAL publish** (darwin Keychain refusal after the file landed; win32 published-path DACL that will not verify after the rename) even though the lane may then hold a working credential. Reason: §2i's Keychain half genuinely makes the lane unusable on darwin; the win32 half is over-conservative on purpose, because the host cannot vouch for that file's ACL. The docstring now says exactly that instead of asserting the lane received nothing — and it is carried forward as a named residual in §8.
20. **The wipe is async, and `PrincipalLaneConsentService.deprovisionLane` and the `accounts.lane.deprovision` handler became async with it.** Reason: §2f/§2i's wipe now reaches the Keychain. No wire shape changed.
21. **The wipe takes the Keychain BEFORE the files** (§2f lists it after the file sweep). Reason: on darwin the Keychain item is what the CLI actually resolves, so removing it first is the fail-closed order; a throw from it stops the wipe being reported done either way.
22. **Round 2's mutation proof 10 is retracted as unreproducible** and replaced by `lane-keychain-scope.test.ts`. Reason: the darwin arm injects `writeKeychain`, so the production default was never executed by any test and the runtime-pair mutation left both files green; the replacement drives the production defaults over a mocked `security` and the mutation was re-run against it.
23. **The wire surface exceeds §3's table by three methods — `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts`, `accounts.lane.statusSubscribe`, `accounts.lane.statusUnsubscribe`** — resolved on the "add the rows" branch: §3 row 12 and an amended row 2 landed in revs 22–23. Reason: the delegable list had no writer at all (§8 item 2) and the subscribe/unsubscribe pair was left implied by "the lane status stream".
24. **§2b's normative "the host never receives the owner's account list" is FALSE in the shipped code.** The ticked delegable list persists host-side in `claudeLaneDelegationRows` (opaque token + owner-authored `displayName` + opted-in email; no credential, no store id). Reason: §2l's phone flow cannot exist without something for the phone to tap. The sentence is corrected and §7 question 11 asks the owner to accept the widening — **the widening ships ahead of that decision**, which is itself a residual.
25. **`accounts.lane.switch_lane_cleared` is one refusal ahead of the §3 table that asserts completeness** (added in rev 23). Reason: an outstanding switch against a lane that was cleared underneath it had no name, and the shared enumeration lets old clients render the host's sentence verbatim.
26. **§2d's peer row enumeration is now exactly closed** — `scope`/`occupied`/`ownerLabel`/`displayName`; `laneState` was dropped from the peer branch. Reason: the one remaining declared peer-visible widening is `displayName`, which §2b/§2k already authorize under Q3.
27. **`ClaudeLaneProjectionRow.laneState` is now optional (self rows only) rather than required.** Reason: a type-level consequence of item 26, not described in the doc.
28. **`LaneStatusStream` takes a `principalOf` lookup in its constructor** (`runtime/lane-status-stream.ts:1-110`); it was stateless in the doc's framing. Reason: a subscriber must be filed by grant and re-resolved per delivery.
29. **`lane-push-envelope.ts` applies BOTH a zod `.max()` (characters) and the UTF-8 byte check.** Reason: §2b's normative bound remains the byte check; the schema bound only moves the rejection earlier.
30. **Shared-lane attribution is NARROWER than §2k's plain "paneKey wins"** (`rate-limits/claude-usage-attribution.ts:105-125`). A shared-lane pane's post is attributed to the shared row only when the posted `configDir` also matches `sharedConfigDir`, and never falls through to a lane row; lane panes keep paneKey-wins unchanged. Reason: `state.claude` is the host-wide, peer-published bar, and a lane is single-account by construction while the shared lane is not — `rememberSharedLane` is rewritten per fetch target (`rate-limits/claude-usage-attribution.ts:68-71`), so the widening let a pane running under another target's dir write the host-wide bar, a post the pre-S9b `!==` dropped.
31. **New refusal `accounts.lane.clear_incomplete`, with a complete two-sentence message.** Reason: §2f requires "the sweep retried rather than reported done" but names no code, and `accounts.lane.clear` needs to fail closed instead of returning `{ cleared }`.
32. **Only the cheap half of §2f's wipe fence landed: re-scan, re-sweep, report only on a clean read-back.** The other half is still S9c and unchanged — the sweep does not confirm the probe's process is dead (`term.kill()` returns before the OS reaps it) and there is no published `laneWipePending` flag.
33. **`isSwitchInProgress` remains host-global in the coordinator** (`claude-accounts/lane-credential-coordinator.ts:73-75`, recorded in code as a deviation). §2f's lane scoping is S9c. Unchanged this round.
34. **`setLaneAccountRowResolvers` accepts `null` for either resolver as an explicit UNSET** (previously `?:` only). Host-local type widening; no wire surface.
35. **Major 3's documentation half was fixed OUTSIDE the pinned worktree**, in `/home/ubuntu/orca-resilience-docs` (revs 22–23). Reason: the task pinned work to the code tree, but the finding's file was this specification and §8 dispositioned both items to that review, so the doc could not be closed from the worktree.

### (c) What the gate skipped, and what is therefore unobserved

- **`typecheck:web` was skipped in every S9b round** under the memory rule (free memory 5518–6097 MB against a 7000 MB floor). No web or renderer file is touched by the credential-lane slices, so the exposure is narrow, but it is unrun.
- **No `describe.runIf(process.platform === 'win32')` test was added for the win32 half of the partial-publish fix.** It is exercised through the injected `WindowsLanePublishOps` on Linux; the real ACL script remains unobserved here. The win32 lane-key-collapse arm in `lane-usage-probe-env.test.ts` is likewise unobservable on this box.
- **The §5 macOS live probe (step 8) is unrun** — no Mac in this sandbox — so darwin lane provisioning stays gated off at `runtime/principal-lane-consent-service.ts`. The Keychain wipe that now landed means the gate no longer hides a write-without-wipe asymmetry when that probe lifts it.
- **The mutation proof for `lane-push-envelope`'s schema `.max()` bound is recorded `observed: false`** — it is behaviourally indistinguishable from the byte check that follows, so no test can go red on it.
- **Mutation proofs for guards landed in earlier rounds of the S9b sub-slice were not re-run** in the final round; only that round's guards and the new join test were mutated.
- **The renderer lane chip is unwired and deferred to S9d, stated in the code** (`TerminalPresenceChip`'s `lane` prop doc and `terminal-lane-account-chip-state.ts`'s header). Reason: the desktop renderer consumes no `terminal.list` lane field anywhere, so wiring the chip means building a per-pane feed of the runtime terminal rows, which §6 budgets as S9d.
- **The `terminal.list` identity proof is schema-shape, not behavioural**, and was left in place rather than deleted; the behavioural half is covered by the two-socket case at `lane-usage-row-isolation.integration.test.ts:115-121`.
